## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request; the reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real); fun's (or why fun cannot answer it); plexsim's verbatim response; why it matters to a real client. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) real behavioural difference — report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the snapshot comparison result.

==== Territory A | web bundle URL templates, mechanically | model=opus | plexsim port 32551 ====

## Your territory: every server path the official web client can build

plexsim serves the official web client, and its bundle is on disk at `/Users/bensarmiento/debridmediamanager/plexsim/webclient/js/` (`main-8792-*.js`, `3115-3115-*.js`, and ~100 `chunk-*.js`). Earlier cycles covered the paths a recorded walk and `probes/ui_surface.py` exercise; nothing has swept the bundle's own URL templates exhaustively. Do that:

1. **Extract** every server-directed path the client can construct from the minified JS: string literals and template/concat pieces beginning with a PMS prefix (`/library`, `/hubs`, `/:/`, `/status`, `/playlists`, `/playQueues`, `/media`, `/system`, `/butler`, `/activities`, `/transcode`, `/video`, `/photo`, `/services`, `/statistics`, `/accounts`, `/devices`, `/myplex`, `/livetv`, `/log`, `/identity`, `/channels`, `/search`, `/security`, `/sync`, `/downloadQueue`, `/updater`, `/prefs`, `/clients`, `/player`, `/resources`, `/servers`, `/music`, `/photos`, …), including forms like `"/library/metadata/".concat(e,"/children")`, `` `/library/sections/${t}/…` `` and query-param builders that attach to them. Record where each came from (file + a short context snippet) and which query params the client attaches. Discard plex.tv / `clients.plex.tv` / `metadata.provider.plex.tv` paths.
2. **Instantiate** each template with real ids on each server (movie, show, season, episode, section, a stream/part id read from a detail row, etc.). Use each server's own ids — a movie template gets kids 4, fun 1116499 and plexsim 1007.
3. **Drop** everything the WRITE_GET guard refuses and anything that is not a GET in the client. Diff the rest across kids, fun and plexsim at port 32551: status, content type, root element, the MediaContainer's attribute-key set, and the child element types (and their attribute-key sets, one level down). Do it once with `Accept: application/json` and once with no Accept header (XML).
4. **Triage** every difference with a hand re-issue on all three servers. Paths plexsim 404s that kids and fun both answer, and paths plexsim answers that both 404, are the highest-value results. Cross-check against `probes/ui_surface.py`'s table so you do not report something it already matches.

==== Territory B | XML and the other client families | model=fable | plexsim port 32552 ====

## Your territory: the XML path, and clients that are not Plex Web

Plex Web asks for JSON. TV, mobile and third-party clients take the DEFAULT format, XML, and python-plexapi parses XML. Everything JSON-shaped has been diffed for seven cycles; the XML path far less.

1. **Replay the recorded corpus as a TV client would.** `tests/fixtures/reference-walk.jsonl` is a real Plex Web session. Replay every GET in it (ratingKeys remapped as `replay_remap.py` does) against kids and plexsim at port 32552 **with the Accept header removed** (and once with `Accept: application/xml`), and diff the XML structurally with `xdiff.py` or your own ElementTree diff: element names, attribute-key sets per element, attribute value classes (int/float/bool/empty), child element order, and the root element. Confirm each candidate on fun.
2. **Extend the python-plexapi corpus.** `handover/tools/probe.py` drove ~460 read calls in cycle 7 (`out_{sim,ref,fun}.json` from that run are gone; re-run it with `PLEXSIM_BASE=http://127.0.0.1:32552`). Read python-plexapi's source (`uv run --no-project --with plexapi python3 -c "import plexapi,os;print(os.path.dirname(plexapi.__file__))"`) and add every READ method and property it did not call — library/section/audio/video/photo/playlist/collection/media/server/settings/client classes; e.g. `section.totalViewSize`, `section.getWebURL` (URL only), `section.searchActors`-style tag searches, `server.library.tags()`, `server.transcodeSessions()`, `server.bandwidth()`, `server.resources()` (statistics), `server.currentBackgroundProcess()`, `server.agents()`, `server.createToken`-free identity reads, `video.augmentation`-free reads, `media.optimizedVersion` reads, `Section.lockAllField`-free field reads… Read each method's source before calling it on kids or fun; if it writes or leaves the host, do not call it there. Diff what plexapi saw across sim/ref/fun with `diff.py` and report any call that raises on plexsim and not on kids and fun, and any attribute or child plexapi reads that kids and fun both send and plexsim omits.
3. **Other client families.** Re-issue a representative set of the corpus reads (home hubs, section grid, preplay for movie/show/season/episode, search, onDeck, related) with the header profile of a non-web client — `X-Plex-Product: Plex for Android (TV)`, `X-Plex-Platform: Android`, `X-Plex-Device: SHIELD Android TV`; `Plex for iOS`/`iOS`; `Plex for Roku`/`Roku`; `Plex for LG`/`webOS` — no Accept header. Does the reference's XML change by client profile (attributes added/removed, different hubs, different `viewMode`/`viewGroup`, different image transcoding)? Where it does, does plexsim change the same way?

## Your territory: what a real PMS writes into Media, Part and Stream for a real file, against what plexsim's `mediainfo.py` writes for the same file

plexsim builds every `Media`, `Part` and `Stream` element from an ffprobe probe (`mediainfo.py`, driven by `build_catalog.py`). Every official client runs its own direct-play gate and draws its quality and track pickers from those attributes BEFORE it asks the server anything. So a wrong or missing attribute fails silently: the client transcodes something plexsim cannot transcode, picks the wrong track, or prints the wrong label. That conversion has only been checked against two `displayTitle` samples and one subtitle-flag sweep. findings.md never mentions Dolby Vision, HDR, audio channel layouts, chroma subsampling or hearing-impaired flags, and `mediainfo._LANGUAGE_NAMES` knows 15 languages. The reference's synthetic clips cannot show any of that; fun's library is real media.

1. **Read** `mediainfo.py` and `build_catalog.py` completely (and wherever `server.py` recomputes stream fields at catalog load): the exact ffprobe invocation — flags, whether any frames are read — and which probe field feeds which attribute. plexsim's ffprobe is `/opt/homebrew/bin/ffprobe` 8.1.2.
2. **Pick a diverse sample on fun** with GETs only. Listing filters narrow it (`?videoCodec=hevc`, `resolution=4k`, `audioCodec=truehd`, `subtitleLanguage=…`, and the like); read each candidate's detail row `GET /library/metadata/{id}` for its Media, Part and Streams. Cover as many of these as fun has, **at most 40 files in total**: H.264 8-bit; HEVC 10-bit SDR and HDR10; Dolby Vision profiles 5, 7 and 8, with and without an HDR10 fallback; HLG; AV1; VC-1 or MPEG-2 if present; containers mkv, mp4, m2ts/ts, avi; audio TrueHD with and without Atmos, E-AC-3 with and without JOC, DTS, DTS-HD MA, DTS:X, AC-3, AAC stereo and 5.1, FLAC, Opus, MP3; several audio tracks including a commentary; subtitles SRT, ASS/SSA, PGS, VobSub, mov_text, with forced, SDH/hearing-impaired and default flags; non-English and uncommon languages, and tracks with no language; track titles; cropped or anamorphic frames (1920x800, 1280x536, 3840x1600); variable frame rate; interlaced; an item with two Media versions; a multi-part item. Choose from the detail rows so each file adds coverage.
3. **Probe each sampled file** with the SAME invocation `build_catalog.py` uses, running the local ffprobe over HTTP against fun's own part URL (`http://fun:32400` + the Part's `key`), with the token sent as a header through ffprobe's `-headers` option, never in a URL. One at a time; kill any probe past 90 s. Save each probe's JSON (it holds no token — check) under your output directory. Probe kids' clips the same way as a second witness.
4. **Convert** each probe with plexsim's own code exactly as `build_catalog.py` does, and **diff** its Media, its Part and every Stream against fun's detail row for the same file, attribute by attribute — presence AND value — in JSON, and in XML (no Accept header) for a handful. Look especially at: `videoResolution` buckets for cropped and anamorphic frames; `videoProfile`, `profile`, `level`, `bitDepth`, `chromaSubsampling`, `chromaLocation`, `colorPrimaries`, `colorRange`, `colorSpace`, `colorTrc`, the `DOVI*` attributes and any dynamic-range field; `frameRate` tokens, `frameRateMode`, `scanType`; `audioChannelLayout`, `channels`, `audioProfile` and a stream's `profile` (e.g. `ma`, `lc`, JOC/Atmos); `bitrate` on Media and on each Stream; `language`, `languageCode`, `languageTag` for every language in the sample; `title`, `displayTitle`, `extendedDisplayTitle` — including the rule cycle 4 left open, when the extended video title appends the profile (`1080p (HEVC Main 10)` on fun versus `1080p (H.264)` on the reference); `default`, `forced`, `hearingImpaired`, `selected`; subtitle `codec` and `format`; `container`, `has64bitOffsets`; `duration` and `size`; stream ORDER and `index`, `streamIdentifier`; Part `videoProfile`/`audioProfile`/`container`/`indexes`.
5. **Classify** every difference: (a) catalog content or a version difference; (b) already recorded above; (c) something PMS derives that the probe does not carry (deep-analysis bitrates, loudness, bitstream-only flags) — an instrument limit, list it but it is not a finding; (d) a real difference plexsim could reproduce from the probe. Report only (d) as findings, each as a RULE with the number of samples supporting it, every counterexample, and at least one verbatim trio: fun's element, the probe fields it maps from, plexsim's element. A rule seen on one sample is a lead, not a finding — say so.
6. **Served rows.** `tests/catalog_ui.json` holds a few items converted from real files. Compare what plexsim at 32582 SERVES for them against fun's row for the same release where fun has it (search fun by title with GETs); the served row passes through the catalog loader's recomputations and can differ from a fresh conversion.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the rule; the samples (fun ratingKey, file container/codecs, never a token or a full debrid URL); fun's verbatim element (trimmed but real); the probe fields; plexsim's verbatim element; why it matters to a real client (which picker, label or direct-play gate reads it). Then how many attributes/samples you triaged into (a), (b), (c), (d) — report only (d) as findings, and list (c) separately. **If you find nothing in (d), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. State how many files you probed on fun. End with the snapshot comparison result.

## Your territory: the XML path of everything cycles 9 and 10 added, and the transport around it

XML is the DEFAULT format a real PMS answers — `_wants_xml()` in plexsim's `server.py` is true unless a client asks for JSON — so it is the path every TV, console and mobile client takes. Cycle 8 batch 2 swept the XML path and found three endpoints rendering `<Video>` where a real PMS renders `<Photo>` or `<Directory>`, because `xmlout` names a `Metadata` row's element from the row's `type` and those rows have none. **Cycles 9 and 10 then added roughly fifty new read endpoints and several new row shapes, and compared every one of them as JSON only.** That is your territory: read-only, mechanical, and entirely on kids and your plexsim.

1. **Enumerate the surface.** Read `docs/findings.md`'s ninth and tenth passes and the cycle 9-10 entries in `HANDOVER.md` §7, and build the list of every endpoint and every row shape those two cycles added or changed — `/library/sections/all`, section `allLeaves`/`albums`/`cluster`/`moment`/`computePath`/`nearest`, `/library/people/{id}` and `/media`, `/library/matches`, `/hubs/items`, `/hubs/metadata/{id}`, `/sorts` by type, `randomArtwork`, a library's `/arts`, `/status/sessions/history/{id}`, the bandwidth and media statistics, the live-TV refusals, season and episode `allLeaves`, voice search, `excludeElements` on a listing, the extras leaf and hub, `/butler` and its tasks, `/library/metadata/{id}/matches`, the artwork lists (`/posters`, `/arts`) for an item, a season, an episode, a collection and a playlist, `/statistics/*`, `/devices`, and everything else those two cycles touched. Fetch each from kids and from plexsim.
2. **Diff the XML mechanically** with no Accept header: root element NAME (two responses on this surface have a root that is not a MediaContainer), every element name (a `Directory` that should be a `Photo`, a `Video` that should be a `Directory`, `Hub`, `Metadata`, `Part`, `Stream`, `Setting`, `ButlerTask`, `Tag`, `Location`, `Field`, `Image`, `UltraBlurColors`, `Preferences`, `Meta`), attribute key sets, attribute VALUE classes (a number rendered as a string, a boolean as `1`/`0` versus `true`/`false`, an absent attribute versus an empty one), CHILD ORDER (plexsim's is `xmlout._CHILD_ORDER`, and `Meta` precedes a listing's rows), and empty body versus an empty container. `handover/tools/xdiff.py` does the structural diff; `cmp.py --xml` fetches.
3. **Values, not just keys.** A key-level comparison alone would have missed `viewMode`, where both sides carry the field and only the values are swapped. Compare values, and say for each difference whether it is catalog content or a rule.
4. **The transport around those responses**: `Content-Type` (and its charset), gzip and the `X-Plex-Content-Original-Length` / `X-Plex-Content-Compressed-Length` pair, `Cache-Control`, `Connection`, and what a HEAD returns for each. Then the paging headers (`X-Plex-Container-Start` / `X-Plex-Container-Size`, both required) on the new containers: cycle 9 established that every MediaContainer pages except a plug-in framework one, so check the ones cycles 9 and 10 added.
5. **Error bodies in XML.** For each new path: a bad parameter, an unknown id, an unknown sub-resource, a wrong verb. What does a real PMS put in the body (and with what Content-Type), and does plexsim match? Do not send a write to kids to produce an error — a bad GET is enough.
6. **python-plexapi as a reader.** `handover/tools/probe.py` drives ~460 plexapi read calls behind a guard that refuses writes; plexapi parses the XML natively, so a wrong element name or a string-where-a-number shows up as an exception or a wrong type rather than a diff. Run it against kids and against your plexsim and compare, with `diff.py`. Read both scripts before running them.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request (method, path, query params in order, the headers that matter); the reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real); plexsim's verbatim response; whether the rule is version-risky (see above); why it matters to a real client. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or a version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference — report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two snapshot comparison results.

## Your territory: every row shape a REAL library shows and a five-clip library cannot

Eleven cycles measured plexsim against a lab of ten synthetic ten-second clips and, for three of them, a production server that is now out of scope. **zen is a real library on the exact version plexsim reports**: 3887 movies, two show libraries, real metadata from real agents -- tags that many items share, real collections, real editions, items with a dozen roles, shows with many seasons, items with several Media versions, unmatched rows, duplicates. Almost every rule in `docs/findings.md` about a ROW's shape was measured where the data was thin. Your job is the shapes that only real content can show.

Read every row you compare from BOTH kids and zen and say which agreed. Where zen has something kids does not (a collection with members, an item with two editions, a tag on 300 items), that is the point -- but a difference that exists only because plexsim's catalog has no such data is catalog content, not a finding. The test is always: *could plexsim produce this from what its own catalog holds?*

1. **Tag `count`.** Cycle 11 saw, and did not pin, that a detail row's tags carry `count` on both servers where plexsim's carry none. Measure it properly: which tag ELEMENTS carry it (Genre, Director, Writer, Producer, Role, Country, Collection, Label, Similar, Network, Guid, Rating), on which ROW shapes (detail, listing, grid, hub, child list, search, tree, a session row), what the number counts (items in the library sharing the tag? in the section? of that type?), and what it is for a tag on exactly one item. Then `/library/sections/{id}/genre` and the other axes, and `/library/tags`, which carry their own counts.
2. **Everything else on a detail row that thin data hides**: `Guid` sets on a well-matched item, `Rating` rows from several agents, `Image` sets, `UltraBlurColors`, `Collection` membership, `Label`, `editionTitle` and two Media versions on one item, `Chapter`/`Marker` where zen has them, `skipParent`, `flattenSeasons`, `showOrdering`, `primaryExtraKey`, `hasPremiumExtras`, `audienceRatingImage`/`ratingImage` pairs, `originalTitle`, `titleSort` on titles with articles in other languages, and every attribute plexsim sends that zen's rows do not carry (a field invented is as wrong as one missing).
3. **A show with real seasons**: `/children`, `/allLeaves`, `/grandchildren`, the All-episodes row's counts, `leafCount`/`viewedLeafCount`/`childCount` arithmetic, a season's own artwork and year, an episode's `parentThumb`/`grandparentArt`, specials (season 0) if zen has any, and a show with more than one Location.
4. **The secondary axes with real data**: `/genre`, `/decade`, `/year`, `/contentRating`, `/resolution`, `/firstCharacter`, `/collection`, `/director`, `/actor`, `/country`, `/label`, `/edition` -- each axis's row shape, its `fastKey`, its counts, its ORDER, and what `?type=` does to it on a library that actually has the rows. Compare against plexsim's for the same axis.
5. **Listing behaviour at scale**: the default order over thousands of rows (ICU collation is deliberately not implemented -- do not report it), `sort=` on every advertised key including the `includeAdvanced` ones, `group=`, the Duplicates filter with real duplicates, `unwatched`/`inProgress` with real watch state, and paging deep into a 3887-row listing.
6. **Search with real content**: `/hubs/search` and `/library/search` against a library where a query matches many rows and several TYPES at once -- the per-hub caps, the `score` values, the tag hubs' `reason`/`reasonID`/`reasonTitle`, and what a two-word query does.

Take a sample and say how large it is. A rule seen on one item is a lead, not a finding.

Cycle 4 deleted the lab's Movies library; cycle 5 changed a show's ordering, which silently re-matched its episodes; cycle 6 left an undeletable download queue. **You are READ-ONLY on kids AND on zen: GET and HEAD only.** zen is production with a real library -- a write there is worse than a write to the lab. ## Your territory: the real library, part two — the shapes a 3887-row library and two real show libraries show that cycle 12 never reached

Cycle 12 was the first cycle pointed at zen, and it took exactly one pass: a movie library's ROW shapes and the search caps. It found seven rules that eleven cycles on a five-clip lab could not have produced. **The rest of that library is untouched.** Your territory is everything about a real library that is not a movie detail row.

The test for a finding is always: *could plexsim produce this from what its own catalog holds?* A difference that exists only because plexsim's catalog has no collections, no editions and no watch history is catalog content, not a finding — but the SHAPE plexsim would have to emit if it did hold them is a finding. Read every row you compare from BOTH kids and zen and say which agreed; kids is 1.43.4 and plexsim reports 1.42.2, so a field only kids sends is a version difference.

1. **A show library with real seasons.** zen has two (`2` Zen Shows and `4` Bench TV), against the lab's single two-season Firefly. Measure `/children`, `/allLeaves`, `/grandchildren` and the All-episodes row on a show with many seasons; `leafCount`/`viewedLeafCount`/`childCount` arithmetic where the numbers are large and partly watched; **season 0 / specials** if zen has any, and where a special sorts; a show with more than one `Location`; `skipParent`, `flattenSeasons`, `showOrdering`, `episodeSort` and `seasonCount` on a real show; a season's own artwork, year and summary against the show's; an episode's `parentThumb`/`grandparentArt`/`parentStudio`/`grandparentSlug` at scale. Then page through a long season list and a long episode list and check the paged projection and the All-episodes row's placement.
2. **Collections with real members.** The lab has none worth the name. On zen: a collection's own detail row and its listing row, `childCount`, `collectionSort`, `collectionMode`, `maxYear`/`minYear`, `subtype`, artwork, `contentRating` and `Label`; `/library/collections/{id}` versus `/library/sections/{id}/collections` versus the `/collection` secondary axis versus a member row's own `Collection` tag; the ORDER a collection's children come back in and what `collectionSort` does to it; a smart collection if zen has one; and which hubs a real library's collections appear in.
3. **Multi-version and multi-edition items.** A row with two `Media` elements: their order, `mediaCount`, `editionTitle`, `Part` ids, which one a decision and a play queue pick, what `excludeElements=Media` leaves, and the Duplicates filter (`includeAdvanced=1`) on a library that has real duplicates. Then `/library/sections/{id}/edition` as an axis.
4. **Listing behaviour at scale.** Paging deep into 3887 rows: `totalSize`/`size`/`offset` at the tail and past the end; every `sort=` key the section advertises including the `includeAdvanced` ones, checked against the rows actually returned rather than against the fact that it answered 200; `group=` over thousands of rows (what the row of a group looks like, what its `size` counts, which fields survive); the `unwatched`, `inProgress` and `unwatchedLeaves` filters where real watch state exists; and the secondary axes (`/genre`, `/decade`, `/year`, `/contentRating`, `/resolution`, `/firstCharacter`, `/director`, `/actor`, `/country`, `/label`, `/studio`) with real rows — each axis's ORDER, its counts, its `fastKey`, and what `?type=` does to it on a library that actually holds rows of that type.
5. **Search where a query matches many rows and several types.** The per-hub caps are recorded; what is not is the ORDER within a hub, the `score` values a real corpus produces, the tag hubs' `reason`/`reasonID`/`reasonTitle` when a query matches a tag AND titles, a two-word query, a query matching a show and its episodes at once, `/library/sections/{id}/search` at scale, and `/library/sections/{id}/autocomplete` against thousands of titles.
6. **Hubs on a real library.** `/hubs`, `/hubs/promoted?contentDirectoryID=` per pinned library, `/hubs/home/recentlyAdded`, the server-wide On Deck and Continue Watching where real watch state exists: which shelves a real library produces, each shelf's `key`/`context`/`style`/`promoted`/`hubKey`, how many rows each carries and in what order, and the row projection per shelf. plexsim's shelf list was measured on a library where most shelves are empty.

Take a sample and say how large it is. A rule seen on one item is a lead, not a finding; a rule seen on twenty is a finding.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request (method, path, query params in order, the headers that matter); each reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real), naming WHICH servers you measured it on; plexsim's verbatim response; why it matters to a real client. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or a version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference -- report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two snapshot comparison results.

## Your territory: the CASCADE family — what a write to a parent does to its children — and the frames nobody has measured

The driver opened this cycle by measuring one write that plexsim gets completely wrong: `PUT /library/metadata/{id}/unmatch` on a SEASON does not touch the season alone, it unmatches **every episode under it** — new `local://` guids, generated titles, `parentGuid` following the season, summaries emptied, people tags dropped, `parentThumb` nulled — while the season loses its year to `parentYear` and falls its thumb back to the show's. plexsim edits the one row and stops. That is your lead: **a write to a parent row is measured on the parent, and the children are never read back.** Find the rest of that family.

Method for every item: build the state identically on both servers, snapshot **the parent AND every descendant** field by field (`/library/metadata/{id}` for each, plus the parent's `/children` and `/allLeaves`), send the write, wait, snapshot again, and diff. Send the identical request to plexsim (ids mapped onto `catalog_ui.json`'s: show `20001`, seasons `30001`/`30002`, episodes `1001`–`1003`, movies `1007`–`1009`) and diff the same way. Read the notification socket (`/:/websockets/notifications`) on both across every write, with at least 10 s of settle. Find the client's exact request in its bundle (`/Users/bensarmiento/debridmediamanager/plexsim/webclient/js/`, searched with python3) before inventing one.

1. **The identity cascades.** `unmatch` on a SHOW (does it reach seasons AND episodes? what does the show itself lose?); `unmatch` on a MOVIE with two Media; `match` applied to a show or a season after an unmatch (does re-identifying a parent re-identify its children, and do they get their old guids back or new ones?); `PUT .../match` on an episode whose season is unmatched; `split` and `merge` on a show or a season and what happens to the descendants' keys. **Do not re-measure a season unmatch's cascade — the driver has it.**
2. **The property cascades.** A show's `showOrdering` write (cycle 5 changed it on the lab by accident and it silently re-matched three episodes — measure that deliberately here, it is safe on the copy); `flattenSeasons`; `episodeSort`; a library's agent or language change; a title/studio/summary edit on a show or a season and exactly which fields land on the children and when; a `.locked=1` on a parent field; an artwork write on a parent and what the children's `parentThumb`/`grandparentThumb` do; a `collectionSort`/`collectionMode` write on a collection with members. Each one: which descendant fields changed, and which frames were pushed for whom.
3. **The delete and trash cascades.** Deleting a season (what the show's `childCount`/`leafCount` do, what frames each row gets, in what order); `emptyTrash`, `clean bundles` and `analyze` on a section versus on an item; a `Delete Version` on a two-Media row. The copy answers 400 to an item DELETE — that is a known instrument limit, so record the frames and the parent arithmetic from whatever DOES work, and say plainly what the limit blocked.
4. **The frames cycle 12 could not reach.** A library create's per-item `created` frames over real files with their activity ids; the nested `provider.subscriptions.process` pairs; the `backgroundProcessingQueue` frame a minute later. Watch the socket for a full 120 s. These are recorded as "captured only in part" twice now — either pin them or say why they cannot be pinned from this instrument.
5. **The writes nobody has sent yet.** Sweep the client bundle for every request template with a method other than GET that `docs/findings.md` does not mention, and measure the ones a client can actually reach: anything under `/library/metadata/{id}/`, `/library/sections/{id}/`, `/playlists/`, `/library/collections/`, `/playQueues/`, `/:/`, `/system/`, `/media/`. Say how many templates you found and how many were already covered.
6. **The XML path of cycle 12's four new writes** — the library create's `Location` header and 201 row, the scan's `?force=1` path, Optimize Database, and the stream-path refusals — sent with no Accept header and with `Accept: application/json`, diffed on root element, element names, attribute key sets and value classes, child order, and empty body versus a container.

## Your territory: the real library, part three — watch state, people, grouping, and the surfaces two passes at zen still never opened

Two passes have now been pointed at zen (cycles 12 and 13) and between them they found 21 rules that eleven cycles on a five-clip lab could not have produced. **Neither pass exhausted it.** Cycle 13's own handover names what it left: `group=` over thousands of rows, the `/library/people` surface at scale, multi-edition playback decisions, and every listing that needs real watch state. That is your starting point, not your limit.

The test for a finding is always: *could plexsim produce this from what its own catalog holds?* A difference that exists only because plexsim's catalog has no collections, no editions and no watch history is catalog content, not a finding — but the SHAPE plexsim would have to emit if it did hold them is a finding. Read every row you compare from BOTH kids and zen and say which agreed; kids is 1.43.4 and plexsim reports 1.42.2, so a field only kids sends is a version difference.

1. **`group=` over thousands of rows.** Cycle 11 recorded which fields 500 and which are 200; nobody has ever looked at what a grouped row actually IS on a library where a group has hundreds of members. For each field that answers 200 (`year`, `decade`, `contentRating`, `studio`, `type`, `guid`, `id`, `titleSort`, `addedAt`, `originallyAvailableAt`, and anything else that is not a tag join, media or per-user state): what the returned row looks like (is it a real Metadata row, a Directory, something else — and if a Metadata row, WHICH member's row is it: first by the current sort, lowest ratingKey, something else?), what the container's `size` and `totalSize` count, whether a `count`/`childCount`/`leafCount` appears, whether `group=` composes with `sort=`, with a filter and with paging, and what `group=decade` does on a real spread of years (the recorded rule is that it groups on the YEAR column and does not collapse by decade — verify that on 3887 rows, because it was measured on five). Then `group=` on a SHOW library and on an EPISODE listing (`type=4`).
2. **`/library/people` and the tag surfaces at scale.** zen's movies carry real cast: 155 roles on one row. Measure `/library/people/{id}` and `/library/people/{id}/media` for an ACTOR with many credits, a DIRECTOR, a WRITER and a PRODUCER: the container's `viewGroup`/`viewMode`/`title1`/`title2`/`thumb`/`art`, the row projection, the ORDER of the rows, paging, `totalSize`, and what the library filters do to it. Then the tag `Directory` rows themselves: `/library/sections/{id}/actor` and `/director` and `/writer` and `/producer` on a real library — their ORDER (alphabetical? by count?), their `count` values against the real number of items, `fastKey`, `thumb`, `key`, and what `?type=` does. And `/library/tags?type=N` at scale.
3. **Every listing that needs real watch state.** zen is production: it has a real view history. `unwatched=1`/`unwatched=0`, `inProgress=1`, `unwatchedLeaves`, `viewedLeafCount`/`viewedOrInProgressLeafCount`/`unviewedLeafCount` as FILTERS and as row fields on a partly-watched show, `lastViewedAt`, `viewCount` and `viewOffset` on real rows, `/library/sections/{id}/onDeck` and the server-wide `/library/onDeck` with real rows in them, Continue Watching, `/library/sections/{id}/recentlyViewed` and `recentlyViewedShows` with real content, and `/status/sessions/history/all` — which answers `size 0` on both servers and has never been explained. **Find the parameter or account state that makes a real PMS list history**: try `accountID=`, `librarySectionID=`, `sort=`, `viewedAt>>=`, a `X-Plex-Account-ID` header, the admin's own account id from `/accounts`, and `/status/sessions/history/all?accountID=1`. If one of them lists rows, that is a finding on its own and it closes a §3.3 entry.
4. **Multi-version and multi-edition rows, read-only.** Cycle 13 could not measure which `Media` a decision picks, because a decision is a write-shaped path. But everything else about a two-Media row is readable: the ORDER of the `Media` elements on the detail row and the grid row, `mediaCount` with `excludeElements=Media`, `editionTitle` on each, the `Part` ids and `key`s, `hasVoiceActivity`/`optimizedForStreaming` per Media, what `duplicate=1` selects at scale (214 movies were counted — check the shape of one), `/library/sections/{id}/edition` as an axis, and whether the two Media's `Stream` lists differ in their `selected`/`default` flags.
5. **Hubs and Home where a real library actually fills them.** plexsim's shelf list was measured where most shelves are empty. On zen (1.42.2, the build that matters): `/hubs` unpaged and paged, `/hubs?count=N`, `/hubs/promoted?contentDirectoryID=` for each pinned library, `/hubs/home/recentlyAdded` with `type=`, the server-wide On Deck, and for EACH shelf: `key`, `context`, `style`, `promoted`, `hubKey`, `hubIdentifier`, `more`, `size`, the row projection, and the ORDER of the rows inside it. Then `/hubs/sections/{id}` and `/hubs/continueWatching` if they exist. Compare shelf-for-shelf with plexsim and say which shelves plexsim builds that zen does not and which zen builds that plexsim does not.
6. **Anything in the first five that turns out to be shallow — keep going into the library.** Deep paging at the tail and past the end; `sort=` keys checked against the rows actually returned rather than against a 200; the secondary axes with real counts; `/library/sections/{id}/firstCharacter` on 3887 titles; `X-Plex-Container-Start` past `totalSize`; a filter that matches exactly zero rows versus one that matches all of them.

Take a sample and say how large it is. A rule seen on one item is a lead, not a finding; a rule seen on twenty is a finding.

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request (method, path, query params in order, the headers that matter); each reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real), naming WHICH servers you measured it on; plexsim's verbatim response; why it matters to a real client. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or a version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference -- report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two snapshot comparison results.

Cycle 11 (74c77e7): the listing query grammar -- `?limit=N` caps a listing's rows (`limit=0` answers ONE row, a negative one every row, `limit=abc` a 500) while `/newest`, `/recentlyAdded` and `/children` ignore it; a `type=` that does not parse is a 500 on a section listing and a 400 on `/library/all` (empty is 500 on both) and a type NUMBER the library holds no rows of answers an empty 200; `id` is a filter field on every listing with integer-versus-text comparison (`=` exact, comma list an OR, `>>`/`<<`/`>=`/`<=` numeric, `==`/`!==` never matching a number, a non-number sorting ABOVE every id) echoed by /query/parse as a Rule with an empty `fieldTitle`; a section's `allLeaves` takes the whole grammar. `excludeElements=` on the section leaves, /library/people/{id}/media and /hubs/items; `includeStorage=1` adding `durationTotal`/`storageTotal` to a section grid row at the library's OWN top-level type only. /library/people/{id}/media typed by the TAG (viewGroup/viewMode for an actor, tagType 6, only) and filtered like a listing. A statistics container paging its SERIES and selecting its Device/Account rows by what the page's rows name; `timespan` validated as a whole number (400) on all three statistics endpoints. gzip on every compressible body whenever the client offers it, with no size floor and no "only if it shrinks" test. A plain queue add landing in Up Next after the block already added (not at the end). The Upload Subtitle modal's `POST /library/metadata/{id}/subtitles?title=&format=` with its 200-empty-body success, its 500/400/406/404 refusals, the sidecar Stream it leaves, the selection it takes and the `<CODEC> External` codec label. `PUT /library/streams/{id}` accepted (200, stores nothing), `DELETE` 403 for an embedded track and 200 for an uploaded sidecar. A playlist's items and a collection's children as the LIGHT grid row. A single `/status/sessions/history/{id}` read projected onto nine fields with `viewType: 1` and no key/ratingKey/thumb.

Cycle 12 (fa48aed): a LIGHT row CAPS its tag lists (2 `Genre`, 2 `Writer`, 2 `Country`, 3 `Role`, always the first of the detail row's order) where the detail row carries the whole list; `/hubs/search` caps at 3 rows per hub with no `limit` and `/library/search` at 10; an actor tag row is `tagType: 6` and a director row 4; an episode's `parentGuid` is the SEASON's guid (corrected at catalog LOAD); the section grid says `allowSync: true`; `group=` takes `type`, `guid` and `id`; `/common` compares an EPISODE's title and not a SEASON's and answers a derived sort title for a single row; `/library/sections/{id}/actor` is the one secondary axis with no `content`. Writes: a subtitle upload pushes NO frames at all; a refresh's `updated 49` frame comes SECOND, before the processing timeline, with the year in its subtitle; a scan ends inside a NESTED `provider.subscriptions.process` pair and `?force=1` runs the whole scan before it refreshes; Optimize Database runs a `general.db.optimize` activity at 0/10/40/60; a library edit needs an `agent`, not a `name`, and a create answers a `Location` header naming the new library; the subtitle upload's format check is CASE-SENSITIVE and takes `psb`; `DELETE /library/streams/abc` is a 400 and `/999999` a 404, and a POST to a stream that exists is a 404; a queue add that adds nothing changes nothing, and a `server://` uri naming another MACHINE adds no rows.

Cycle 14, measured by the driver BEFORE this hunt and being implemented right now -- do not re-report: (a) the light row's `Director` cap is **3** and `Producer` is absent from a light row entirely (measured on zen across movie, show and episode rows; kids has no row rich enough); (b) `PUT /library/metadata/{id}/unmatch` on a SEASON CASCADES to every episode under it -- each episode's guid becomes `local://<id>`, its title the generated `Episode N`, its `parentGuid` follows the season to `local://<season id>`, its `summary` is emptied, its `year`, `audienceRating` and people tags are dropped, its `titleSort` is removed and its `parentThumb` becomes null -- while the season itself loses its `year` (gaining `parentYear` from the show), falls its `thumb` back to the SHOW's thumb and keeps its own `parentGuid` and external `Guid` array; (c) the `<CODEC> External` sidecar-subtitle label, now confirmed on zen as well as the copy.

Cycle 13 additions — do not re-report (24 commits, all on `main` at 5a07a1c): a row with NO value sorts BELOW every value on every sort key and row type (twelve of the fifteen advertised sorts default to `desc`); a light row caps `Director` at THREE and carries no `Producer` at all; `/hubs/search` returns the ITEMS behind a matched tag, each naming it in `reason`/`reasonID`/`reasonTitle`, while `/library/search` carries the tag rows and title hits only; the seventeen search hubs' base ORDER (`movie` fifth, `collection` fourteenth); the "All episodes" row follows the PAGE's row count, not the offset — present whenever a page returns more than one season row, wherever it starts, gone on a one-row page even at `Start=0`; `duplicate=1` is "more than one Media", and on a SHOW or SEASON "a leaf has more than one"; a bare `<field>=` means three different things (a tag-TABLE field matches NOTHING, `studio`/`editionTitle` match EVERY row, everything else matches the rows with no value) and `contentRating=None` in that spelling is the empty synonym and the key of that axis's trailing bucket; a tag or field axis REFUSES an unparseable `type=` (500, or 400 on `year`) where a MEDIA axis ignores `type=` completely; an EPISODE with an `audienceRating` carries `audienceRatingImage` and a `Rating` row and a SEASON never does; a shelf with no `count=` carries SIX rows and says `more`; 1.42.2 builds three shelves 1.43.4 does not (`movie.topunwatched`, `tv.startwatching`, `tv.toprated`); `pinnedContentDirectoryID` reshapes the promoted Recently Added shelf into `home.movies.recent.N` on 1.42.2 and changes nothing on 1.43.4; unmatching a SEASON unmatches every episode under it and unmatching a SHOW takes its seasons AND episodes, with every descendant's `parentGuid`/`grandparentGuid` following; an episode unmatch drops the people lists, `audienceRating` and `year` and empties `summary` by either path; an unmatch leaves NO activity running; a season's `index` lands on its episodes' `parentIndex`.

## Your territory: build the collection instrument, put a leaf in progress, and measure the writes no cycle has reached

Cycle 13 left three named holes and all three are yours. **The first two are things no reference HOLDS, so the instrument has to be built before the measurement exists.** plexscratch is disposable and its writes are free: that is exactly what it is for.

1. **Build a collection instrument, then measure collections properly.** Neither kids nor zen holds a single collection — `/library/sections/{id}/collections` is `size 0` on both — so `collectionSort`, `collectionMode`, `minYear`/`maxYear`, `subtype`, member order and the collection hubs have **never been measured at all**. On the copy: create several collections (a movie one with 3+ members, a show one, one with a single member, a SMART one) and then measure everything about them. The detail row and the listing row and how they differ; `childCount`, `maxYear`/`minYear`, `subtype`, `collectionSort`, `collectionMode`, `contentRating`, `Label`, `thumb`/`art`, `addedAt`/`updatedAt`; `/library/collections/{id}` versus `/library/sections/{id}/collections` versus the `/collection` secondary axis versus a member row's own `Collection` tag — the four disagree and nobody has written down how; the ORDER a collection's children come back in and what each `collectionSort` value does to it (0, 1, 2 — and what an unknown one does); `collectionMode` values and what each does to the library grid and to the show page; adding and removing members and what `PUT /library/collections/{id}/items` accepts; the frames every one of those writes pushes; renaming; and which hubs a library's collections appear in once some exist. Also: does a collection appear in `/library/all`, in the Duplicates filter, in `/hubs/search`, in `/library/search`, and what `type=18` answers on a library that holds them.
2. **Put a leaf in progress and settle `viewedOrInProgressLeafCount`.** Neither reference holds a leaf that is in progress and unwatched, so that field is indistinguishable from `viewedLeafCount` on both and plexsim just reads it as the name says — the one loose end cycle 13 left in the code. On the copy, with `long` media: send a timeline that leaves one episode at, say, 40% (well under the 90% completion threshold and well over the 60 000 ms minimum), then read the SHOW and the SEASON rows back and compare `leafCount`, `viewedLeafCount`, `viewedOrInProgressLeafCount` and `unviewedLeafCount`, and run the three as FILTERS on the show listing. Then watch one episode to completion as well and read all four again, so there is a row with one watched, one in progress and one untouched. Also check `inProgress=1` as a filter, On Deck's choice with a leaf in progress, and whether a `/:/progress` write (which never completes anything) counts as "in progress" the same way a timeline does.
3. **Re-measure the six cascades cycle 13 recorded and did NOT implement**, because they are single-witness on the copy and the driver never verified them — and the driver is implementing them from your report this cycle, so a wrong one costs a real bug. For each: the exact request, the frames in order with their timings, and the rows before and after. (a) a show REFRESH and a season REFRESH cascading through the tree (40 and 20 frames were counted); (b) `flattenSeasons` and `episodeSort` pushing NOTHING while changing the rows — confirm exactly which row fields change (`skipChildren` on the show, `skipParent` and no `parentRatingKey` on every episode, the season's `/children` order) and confirm the socket really is silent; (c) an artwork PICK (`PUT /library/metadata/{id}/poster?url=`) re-versioning `thumb`, `art` and `theme` together, cascading to children, LOCKING the field, one frame at +5 s; (d) `PUT /library/metadata/{id}/analyze` pushing a per-level triple plus a `media.generate.chapter.thumbs` activity; (e) the library-create sequence's 74 frames and the `backgroundProcessingQueue` regeneration at +60 s — and specifically whether that regeneration frame is periodic rather than create-specific (it was seen 8 s after an unrelated edit); (f) the merge/split arithmetic on a SHOW tree, where plexsim's season merge also deletes the season's EPISODE. (f) is a data-loss bug in plexsim and the most valuable of the six: measure exactly what a real PMS does to both trees on a season merge and on the split that follows.
4. **Whatever the first three leave time for: the writes no cycle has reached.** Candidates, in order: `PUT /library/sections/{id}/all` with `type=` scoping on a SHOW library (the item path and the section path disagree everywhere else); label writes (`label[0].tag.tag=`, `label[].tag.tag-=`) and the `/label` axis they feed; `collectionSort` and `collectionMode` as ITEM prefs; `PUT /library/metadata/{id}/prefs`; a playlist of MIXED types; `POST /playlists/upload`; `PUT /playlists/{id}/items/{id}/move?after=` at the head and tail; `DELETE /library/metadata/{id}` on a SEASON and on a SHOW and what it does to the tree; `emptyTrash` and `clean` after such a delete; `PUT /library/sections/{id}/refresh?force=1` against a library whose files have changed; and the frames each pushes.

For every write: send it, capture the frames on the notification socket in order with their offsets, read the rows back BEFORE and AFTER, and say what plexsim does for the same request. A frame sequence you did not watch on the socket is a guess — cycle 11 invented a subtitle-upload frame pair that way and cycle 12 had to remove it.

## Report format

Most important first. Also `inProgress=1` as a filter, and whether a `/:/progress` write counts as in-progress the same way a timeline beat does.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request (method, path, query params in order, the headers that matter); each reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real), naming WHICH servers you measured it on; plexsim's verbatim response; **what it costs a real client** (what breaks, and for whom) -- a finding that changes nothing any client can see should say so; and which of the six failure classes in HANDOVER.md 3.1 your own measurement could belong to, and why it does not. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or a version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference -- report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two snapshot comparison results.

## Your territory: the real library, part four -- and the surface swept by SHAPE rather than by path

Three passes at zen have now found 29 rules that a five-clip library cannot show, and **not one of them exhausted it**. You are read-only on both references, and zen is the instrument that matters: 3,887 movies, two real show libraries, real collections, real editions, real tags with real counts.

Cycle 14's most productive hour was not a hunt at all -- it was a mechanical sweep that fetched every path from kids, from zen and from plexsim and reported only where the two references AGREE and plexsim differs. That filters every version difference out automatically. It found five findings, three of them endpoints plexsim had invented. **Do that again, but sweep a dimension it did not.** Candidates, in order of expected yield:

1. **Sweep by PARAMETER, not by path.** Cycle 14 swept paths with no query at all. Take the twenty or so listing and item paths that matter and cross them with the parameters the client actually sends -- `includeGuids`, `includeConcerts`, `includeExtras`, `includeOnDeck`, `includePopularLeaves`, `includeMarkers`, `includeChapters`, `includeStations`, `includeExternalMedia`, `includeRelated`, `includeRelatedCount`, `includeReviews`, `includeLoudnessRamps`, `includeBandwidths`, `includeCollections`, `includeFields`, `excludeFields`, `checkFiles`, `asyncCheckFiles`, `asyncAugmentMetadata`, `asyncRefreshAnalysis`, `asyncRefreshLocalMediaAgent` -- on a row type rich enough to answer them. HANDOVER says each `include*`/`exclude*` DOES something and that ignoring one answers a valid document with a block missing; only a handful have ever been measured, and zen has rows rich enough to show the rest.
2. **The show libraries at scale.** Two passes went at zen's movies; "Zen Shows" and "Bench TV" have had far less. Deep paging through a show grid, a season grid and an episode grid; the leaf-count columns on rows that actually differ; `/allLeaves` on a show with many seasons; the episode-list `Meta` and its sorts; `absoluteIndex` where it is real; multi-season On Deck and Continue Watching with real watch state; `firstCharacter` on a show library; the show-library secondary axes against the movie ones.
3. **Real collections, read-only.** zen holds real collections, which the lab does not. Everything a collection row carries in a real library -- in `/library/all?type=18`, in the section grid, in the `/collection` axis, as a member's own `Collection` tag, in the hubs, in search -- against what plexsim answers for its own. (The WRITE half of collections belongs to the other hunter; stay read-only and stay out of its way.)
4. **Real editions and real duplicates.** zen has multi-edition items and 214 movies with more than one `Media`. `editionTitle`, the Duplicates filter, which `Media` a listing row carries and in what order, `mediaCount`, `includeStorage` sums, and how a two-Media row's Part list is shaped.
5. **Anything the parameter sweep turns up.** Follow it rather than this list.

Send paging headers on every listing. Compare THREE servers and report only where kids and zen AGREE and plexsim differs; where the two references disagree, that is a version difference and plexsim follows **zen**, which is the build it reports.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact request (method, path, query params in order, the headers that matter); each reference's verbatim response (trimmed but real), naming WHICH servers you measured it on; plexsim's verbatim response; **what it costs a real client** (what breaks, and for whom) -- a finding that changes nothing any client can see should say so; and which of the six failure classes in HANDOVER.md 3.1 your own measurement could belong to, and why it does not. Then how many requests/paths you triaged as (a) catalog content or a version difference, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference -- report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two snapshot comparison results.


==== Cycle 16 | Territory A | the write surface below the collection -- playlists, the play queue, the show-library save and item prefs | plexsim port 32584 | WRITES to plexscratch ====

NOTE ON THIS ENTRY: cycle 16's scratch directory was cleared mid-cycle to recover disk
space, taking the two assembled prompt files with it. The shared preamble, the
"Already fixed", "Measured and deliberately NOT implemented", SAFETY, instrument and
report-format sections were cycle 15's, refreshed exactly as section 5 step 4 describes
(cycle 15's 33 findings added to the fixed list, its eight not-implemented items and
three refutations added to the second). Only the TERRITORY text is reproduced below, and
it is verbatim.

## Your territory: the write surface BELOW the collection -- playlists, the play queue, the show-library save, and item prefs

Cycle 15 finished the collection surface and swept the verb table. **Everything one level below it has had one pass in cycle 9 and nothing since**, and cycle 9 is the cycle whose findings section 3.3 flags as thinnest: seven of them were refuted on re-measurement, and every write rule from cycles 9-12 has plexscratch as its only possible witness. Your job is to re-measure that layer properly and to reach the parts of it nobody has.

Take these in order. Each is a cycle-sized territory on its own, so depth beats breadth -- a finding measured three ways is worth five asserted once.

1. **Playlists, end to end.** They were measured once, in cycle 9, and the surface has grown since. Build several on the copy (a movie playlist with 4+ items, a show/episode one, a MIXED one if a real PMS allows it, a SMART one, one with a custom title and summary, an empty one if that is possible) and settle, measuring each against plexsim: the create grammar and every refusal (`title`, `type`, `uri`, a type that does not fit the uri, a `uri` naming another machine, a uri naming a whole library, a comma-joined key list, a mix of types); `POST /playlists/{id}/items?uri=` -- where a plain add LANDS (head, tail, or after a block), what a duplicate add does, what adding an item already in the list does, and whether the order the uri names is preserved; `PUT /playlists/{id}/items/{itemID}/move?after=` at the HEAD (no `after`), at the TAIL, to itself, and `after=` an id that is not in the list; `DELETE /playlists/{id}/items/{itemID}` and what happens to the other items' `playlistItemID`s; `PUT /playlists/{id}?title=&summary=` and which fields are editable; `GET /playlists/{id}/items` with the whole listing grammar on it (`type=`, a filter, `sort=`, `limit=`, paging, `excludeElements=`, `includeMeta=1`); `/playlists/all`, `/playlists?playlistType=`, `/playlists?type=`, `/playlists/upload`; a playlist's `composite`, `thumb` and artwork writes; `duration`, `leafCount`, `viewCount` and `addedAt`/`updatedAt` on the row as items come and go; and every frame each write pushes (type 15, in order, with offsets). Then the SMART half: what `PUT /playlists/{id}/items?uri=<listing>` really stores, what a smart playlist refuses, and what `content` and `leafCount` are derived from on each read.
2. **The play queue's mutations.** Cycle 4 built it, cycle 11 corrected its add rule after SEVEN cycles of a wrong proxy, and nothing has swept it since. On the copy, with a real queue over a show and over a playlist: `PUT /playQueues/{id}/items/{itemID}/move?after=` in every position; `DELETE /playQueues/{id}/items/{itemID}` for the selected item, the first, the last and an unknown id; `PUT /playQueues/{id}?repeat=&shuffle=&continuous=`; `POST /playQueues?playlistID=` and `?uri=` in every form the client builds; what `playQueueVersion`, `playQueueSelectedItemID`, `playQueueSelectedItemOffset`, `playQueueSelectedMetadataItemID`, `playQueueLastAddedItemID` and `playQueueTotalCount` do on each; `GET /playQueues/{id}?own=1&includeBefore=&includeAfter=` (the window the client actually reads); `/playQueues/{id}/refresh`; and what a queue does when an item it holds is DELETED or edited underneath it. Report the exact attribute set of the container on each mutation.
3. **`PUT /library/sections/{id}/all` on a SHOW library.** HANDOVER records that the section save path is scoped by `type` AND by library, that a type-scoped filter there is a 400, and that the save MERGES tags where the item path REPLACES them -- all measured on a MOVIE library. Nobody has run it on a show library. Do the whole grammar there: `type=2` (show), `type=3` (season), `type=4` (episode); which fields are editable at each type; what a tag write does at each; whether a parent save CASCADES the copied `parentTitle`/`grandparentTitle`/`parentStudio` onto the children (HANDOVER says they must be copied again on every edit -- prove it for the section path); the frame set, in sort order, for a save that touches several rows; and what an `id=` naming rows of a different type or a different library does.
4. **The item-prefs family.** `PUT /library/metadata/{id}/prefs?...` was measured for five settings on a SHOW. Sweep the rest: every setting `GET /library/metadata/{id}?includePreferences=1` advertises for a MOVIE (3), a SHOW (11) and a SEASON (3); what each write does to the ROW and to the CHILDREN (cycle 15 found `flattenSeasons`/`episodeSort` are a tri-state where `-1` inherits and that the row effects are real); which push frames and which push none (**with a control write first**); validation per type (an unknown id, a bad int, a bool that is not `1`/`true`, an empty value); and the read-back type of each.

For every write: send it, capture the frames on the notification socket in order with their offsets, read the rows back BEFORE and AFTER, and say what plexsim does for the same request. **A frame sequence you did not watch on the socket is a guess** -- cycle 11 invented a subtitle-upload frame pair that way and cycle 12 had to remove it. And a "zero frames" measurement needs a CONTROL: run a write that MUST push frames (a title edit pushes `state 3` then `state 5`) on the same socket first, or a broken listener reads exactly like a silent server. **A hunter's rule is scoped to what it tried**: cycle 15's "a show's prefs write pushes nothing" was true of four settings and false of `showOrdering`, and a standing suite check caught it. Re-measure any rule you are about to generalise on a case you did NOT use to find it.

==== Cycle 16 | Territory B | the HTTP envelope -- request headers, response headers, and the XML half | plexsim port 32586 ====

## Your territory: the HTTP envelope -- what a request's HEADERS do, and what every response's headers say

Two mechanical sweeps have each produced a batch of findings that no amount of re-hunting the previous dimension would have: cycle 14 swept the surface by PATH and found three endpoints plexsim had INVENTED; cycle 15 swept it by METHOD (33 paths, every one different) and by PARAMETER (`excludeElements=` turned out to belong to the RESPONSE, not to eight hand-wired handlers). **The dimension nobody has swept is the HTTP envelope itself.** Everything plexsim knows about headers it learnt from three bugs -- `Accept` negotiation, gzip, and CORS -- and each of those was found by accident.

Sweep it properly, comparing THREE servers and reporting only where kids and zen AGREE and plexsim differs. That filters out every version difference automatically.

1. **Request headers that carry meaning.** For a representative set of ~25 paths across the surface (a section grid, an item detail, `/children`, `/hubs/promoted`, `/hubs/search`, `/library/sections`, `/:/prefs`, `/identity`, `/playlists`, `/status/sessions`, `/photo/:/transcode`, an artwork path, `/library/all`, `/filters`, a secondary axis, `/query/parse`, a subset listing, `/system/agents`, `/media/providers`, `/` ...), vary ONE header at a time and record what changes in the body, the status and the response headers: `Accept` (every form cycle 5 did not try -- `*/*`, `text/xml`, `application/xml;q=0.9, application/json;q=0.8`, a malformed one, several ranges, no Accept at all); `Accept-Encoding` (`gzip`, `deflate`, `br`, `zstd`, `identity`, `gzip;q=0`, a malformed one, absent); `Accept-Language` (does anything localise?); `X-Plex-Container-Start`/`-Size` (already known, but check which paths read them from headers ONLY and which also read the query); **`X-Plex-Platform`, `X-Plex-Product`, `X-Plex-Device`, `X-Plex-Client-Identifier`, `X-Plex-Version`, `X-Plex-Model`, `X-Plex-Features`, `X-Plex-Language`, `X-Plex-Session-Identifier`, `X-Plex-Provides`, `X-Plex-Text-Format`, `X-Plex-Drm`, `X-Plex-Token`** on paths that have never been asked for them (HANDOVER records that the codec suffix in `displayTitle` is keyed on thirteen PRODUCTS and that iOS types "All episodes" as a season -- those two are known; what ELSE is keyed on a client header?); `Range` and `If-None-Match`/`If-Modified-Since` on artwork and on containers; `Origin` and `Access-Control-Request-Headers` (the CORS echo is implemented -- check the preflight's full shape, `Access-Control-Max-Age`, `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` and what a non-preflight cross-origin GET gets back); `Connection`, `Expect: 100-continue`, `Content-Type` on a GET, a duplicated header, a header with no value, and an absurdly long one.
2. **RESPONSE headers of every route.** For every path in the surface (cycle 14's sweep listed 59 top-level paths and 48 item leaves -- reuse that list, and read `server.py`'s route table for the rest), record the FULL response header set from kids, from zen and from plexsim, and diff them: which headers each sends, in what ORDER, with what values and what capitalisation. `Content-Type` and its charset per path and per format; `Cache-Control` per path (HANDOVER says `no-cache` on every response and `max-age=259200` on transcoder output only -- verify, including artwork, media bytes, `/web/*`, an error and a 404); `Content-Length` versus `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` and which paths stream; `X-Plex-Content-Original-Length`/`-Compressed-Length` (on which responses exactly?); `X-Plex-Protocol`; `Connection`; `Date`; `ETag`/`Last-Modified`/`Expires`/`Vary`/`Age`/`Accept-Ranges`; **`Server:` -- plexsim sends `PlexSim/0.1 Python/3.10.20` and a real PMS sends none** (that one is already known and the driver is handling it -- but say whether any OTHER banner-shaped header differs); and the header set on a 400, a 401, a 404, a 406, a 500 and a redirect.
3. **The XML half of everything cycles 14 and 15 added.** XML is the DEFAULT response format, not JSON, and a JSON-only check proves nothing about the path a TV or mobile client takes -- three endpoints were once rendering `<Video>` where a real PMS renders `<Photo>` or `<Directory>`. Cycles 14 and 15 added ~62 findings and most were measured in JSON. Take the new surface -- `/userRating` and the nine empty axes of other library types, `/banners`, `/thumbs`, the `Meta` on `/playlists` and the Recently Added hub, the inline `Children` and `Related` blocks, the collection detail row and its `includePreferences`, the light section-search row, `excludeElements=` on every container that carries rows, the new Rule shapes from `/query/parse`, the history listing with its `type=`, the artwork DELETE, the verb refusals and the 406 -- and diff its XML against both references: element NAMES, the child element ORDER (`xmlout._CHILD_ORDER`), which attributes are present, attribute VALUE formatting (booleans as `1`/`0` versus `true`/`false`, floats, empty strings, negative numbers), and whether an error body is XML at all.

**Report only where kids and zen AGREE and plexsim differs.** A header only kids sends is a version difference and not a finding, because plexsim reports 1.42.2. Say for every rule which servers you measured it on, and how many paths/headers you swept in each of the three parts.

`group=` reaches the section `allLeaves` while an ITEM's `/allLeaves?group=` is ignored; `#` is the DIGITS bucket of `firstCharacter` and any other non-letter gets a bucket of its OWN listed after Z, every bucket key percent-encoded; `/library/sections/{id}/userRating` is a real axis with a FIXED six-bucket scale (`10` "5 stars" down to `2` "1 star" plus `-1` "Not rated") that IGNORES `type=`; the nine axes of OTHER library types (`mood`, `style`, `format`, `subformat`, `source`, `make`, `model`, `place`, `tag`) answer an EMPTY secondary container with their own `title2` rather than a 404, and they VALIDATE `type=` the way `/genre` does; `/library/metadata/{id}/banners` and `/thumbs` are real candidate lists (200 on both versions) while `/squareArts` is 1.43.4-only; `/library/sections/{id}/recentlyReleased`, `/library/metadata/{id}/background` and `/library/metadata/{id}/composite` are 404s on both references and plexsim had INVENTED all three; On Deck is the MOST RECENTLY VIEWED in-progress episode, not the lowest-numbered; an artwork PICK re-versions `thumb`, `art` and `theme` onto ONE stamp, moves `updatedAt`, LOCKS the picked field, pushes one frame (state 5, no `metadataState`) and CASCADES the stamp to the children that borrow it; a hub container says `allowSync: true` on every hub path except `/hubs/metadata/{id}/postplay`, which says false, while `/hubs/search` and `/hubs/items` carry none; on a SEASON listing the leaf-count filters read the SHOW's counts; a collection answers `/children` on the METADATA path as a full secondary container (with `key`, `parentIndex` = its TAG id, `parentTitle`, `title2`, `nocache` and the librarySection triple) while `/library/collections/{id}/children` answers the BARE `{size: N}`, a `type=18` listing is a real QUERY, a collection row's `index` is its own tag id, and `/library/all?type=18` lists every collection the server holds across libraries in title order; the prefs cascade belongs to `showOrdering` ALONE (45 frames) while `flattenSeasons`, `episodeSort`, `useOriginalTitle` and `subtitleMode` push ZERO frames and change the ROWS instead (`flattenSeasons=1` sets `skipChildren` on the show and `skipParent` on every episode while REMOVING their `parentRatingKey`/`parentKey`; `episodeSort=1` puts a season's `/children` newest first while `/allLeaves` keeps broadcast order); a merge whose TARGET has no `Media` of its own (a season, a show) absorbs NOTHING -- a 200, no frames, the tree unchanged -- and the `split` after it is a no-op too.

Cycle 15, measured by the DRIVER before this hunt and being implemented right now -- do not re-report: (a) `titleSort`'s whitespace trim is LEFT-ONLY, not `.strip()` -- a title written with a trailing space keeps it in `titleSort` (`'  Zeta Both  '` sorts as `'Zeta Both  '`, `'The Zeta Art '` as `'Zeta Art '`) while leading whitespace goes; (b) the SECTION-level `flattenSeasons`/`episodeSort` produce the SAME row effects as the item path, and the ITEM pref is a tri-state where `-1` means INHERIT the section's value and `0`/`1` override it in both directions; (c) the `group=` `addedAt` tie-break is the LOWEST ratingKey and is sort-independent (settled on the copy, where movies 1 and 17 share `addedAt` 1789187071) -- plexsim was already right.

Cycle 16 (49 findings, all landed on `main` -- **do not re-report any of these**):
`/library/sections/{id}/firstCharacter` counts a row BELOW the library's top-level type by its SHOW's sort title while the LISTING behind each bucket filters on the row's OWN stored sort title, so a season list's `A` bucket can say 64 and answer 0 rows; `/firstCharacter/{key}` is a real listing (`title2` = `Start with '<key>'`, the key echoed as sent, `%23` decoded, the light grid row, the whole listing grammar on top); **every** section leaf's extra path segment is that leaf's BUCKET or a 404 and never the leaf again (`/all/zzz`, `/onDeck/zzz`, `/sorts/zzz`, `/studio/<key>`, `/audioLanguage/en`, `/location/1` and fourteen more are 404s; a tag axis is keyed by TAG ID, `year`/`decade` by a non-negative integer with `decade/1995` a ten-year window, `contentRating` as `Rated <v>`, `resolution` accepting anything, `userRating` as `<n> Star` refusing `-1`); a show library's `?type=3`/`?type=4` grid defaults to BROADCAST order (show sort title, `parentIndex`, `index`); a show library's `/unwatched` carries `nocache`; an artwork path is a 200 only on the row that OWNS the image and a borrower carries the owner's path verbatim.
A real PMS LOCALISES its own text: `X-Plex-Language` (header or query) beats `Accept-Language`, a regional form works like its primary subtag, an unknown language falls back to English, 28 languages answer differently, and the filter/sort menus, `Meta` type titles, hub titles, a GENERATED `Season N`, a stream's language name and `displayTitle` and all 200+ `/:/prefs` strings move while the LIBRARY's own rows, `All Movies`, an axis's `title2` and `Unplayed` do not -- the table is keyed by ATTRIBUTE and then by the English string (`translations.json`).
A real PMS ROUTES before it authenticates: with no token an unknown path is a 404, exactly eleven single-segment paths are 401 (`library hubs playlists devices butler servers clients accounts activities playQueues log`), `/identity` and `/security` are 200 and every other one is a 403; `GET /` is a 401 with a 193-byte body that walks a browser into the web client.
Every response names its connection state (`Connection: Keep-Alive` + `Keep-Alive: timeout=20`, and an idle socket really is closed at 20 s); a paged container answers `X-Plex-Container-Start`/`-Total-Size` as HEADERS and the paging HEADER beats the query per parameter (a header `Start=abc` beside a valid query is a 500); `Range` applies to EVERY response body (206/416, an unrecognised unit read as bytes, a 206 never gzipped); a malformed request header is a 400 (no colon, a TAB in a value, a name over 1024, a value over 4096); the Accept `q` grammar RANKS a zero rather than dropping it (a range qualifies only if its type is exactly `application/json`/`application/xml` and it has no parameters or a first parameter beginning exactly `q=`; no parameter weighs 1.0, a single parseable non-negative q weighs that, everything else weighs 0 and still qualifies); `Accept-Encoding` tokens match exactly and case-sensitively (`GZIP`, `x-gzip` and `*` get plain; `gzip;q=0` is still gzipped); no `100 Continue`; a preflight with no `Origin` carries no CORS headers; `/web` and `/web/` are a `302 Moved Temporarily` with an ABSOLUTE Location from the request's own `Host` and `Cache-Control: public` (the one non-`no-cache` place on the surface) while every `/web/*` asset is `no-cache`, `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, never gzipped, `.js` is `text/javascript`, `.html` carries no charset and `.ico` is `application/octet-stream`; a section's bare `/composite` is a 404 and only `/composite/{version}` answers; `X-Plex-Platform: Roku` strips `id` and `Pivot` from every library `Directory` of `/media/providers`; a `Meta/Type` orders its children `Filter, Sort, Field`, `includeDetails=1` carries no `active`/`activeDirection` on a Sort nor `advanced` on a Filter where `includeMeta=1` carries all three, the rows `includeChildren=1` inlines carry no librarySection triple, and a MediaContainer's attributes come out `size`, then `totalSize`/`offset` when paged, then alphabetically; `POST /library/metadata/{id}/extras` is a 400 whatever the body; **a real PMS sends no `Server:` banner at all**.
`/playlists/{id}/items` is a real typed QUERY (`type=2` answers the SHOW owning the member episodes, `type=3` their seasons, `-1` everything, `1.5` a 400; the library filters apply; `sort=`/`limit=` are parsed then ignored and an unparseable one is a 500) and `?includeMeta=1` carries the page's own tab bar; a playlist is born `viewCount: 1` with `lastViewedAt == addedAt` and the pair is on the LISTING row only; `/playlists` filters by `title`, `smart`, `sectionID` and `type` (15; `type=abc` is a 500) and its default order is SMART first then `titleSort`; `sort=mediaCount` is real there and `leafCount` a 500; `POST /playlists/{id}/items` is a 404 (only PUT adds); an empty `title=` is stored verbatim; `smart=1` over a comma-joined KEY LIST is a 400 (the test is the uri's SHAPE, never whether it resolves); a uri the server cannot resolve adds NOTHING; `PUT /playlists/{id}` takes the whole ITEM edit grammar (thumb/art mint artwork under `/library/metadata/{playlist ratingKey}/`, every `.locked` locks, `year`/`originallyAvailableAt`/`studio` store, `userRating`/`guid`/`duration`/timestamps are accepted and discarded); `playlistItemID` is a SERVER-WIDE sequence and a smart list's items carry none (every item write on one is a 400); a playlist's artwork lives under `/library/metadata/{ratingKey}/` (posters, arts, thumbs, banners, tree all 200) while `/playlists/{id}/{posters,arts,thumb,art}` and the bare `composite` are 404s.
A shuffled play queue is really SHUFFLED (plexsim reversed it deterministically); `includeBefore`/`includeAfter` window the queue with NO `window=` (unlimited radius) and each is a boolean whose every other value is a 400; EVERY item delete answers the container as it stood BEFORE it and only the SELECTED one also drops the rows; a move refuses `after=<itself>` (404) and an empty `after=` (400); a queue built from a PLAYLIST uri carries `playQueuePlaylistID`/`playQueuePlaylistTitle` and no `playQueueSourceURI`.
The SECTION save path stores an unlocked `titleSort.value=` until the next TITLE write re-derives it (where the ITEM path discards it); a show's `contentRating` reaches only the episodes carrying none of their own; a show's `index` cascades onto its seasons' `parentIndex`; a season that LOSES its year gains `parentYear` in the same read; `type=abc` on the save path is a 400; an UNMATCHED item drops its agent-driven prefs (a show loses `languageOverride` and `useOriginalTitle`, a MOVIE loses the whole `Preferences` block, a season keeps its three); and on the section save path the `state 3` frame carries the row's title from BEFORE the write while `state 5` carries the new one.

A rule is a finding when BOTH answer it; a field only kids sends is a version difference and not a finding, because plexsim reports 1.42.2. Say for every rule which servers you measured it on.
