## Your territory: the CROSS-ENDPOINT dimension -- what a write changes everywhere ELSE

Every write cycle of this programme has asked two questions: *what does this write ANSWER*, and *what FRAMES does it push*. Both are well covered. **Nobody has ever asked the third: which OTHER endpoints change, and how.**

That is the question a real client actually depends on. The UI does not read the row it just wrote -- it reads the Home screen's hubs, the Continue Watching shelf, the section's Recently Added, the On Deck list, the history page, the parent's leaf counts, the collection's members, the filter axes, the `Meta` descriptor, `/library/sections` itself. A write that lands correctly on its own row and leaves every derived view stale is invisible to every check this project has ever run, and breaks the screen the user is actually looking at.

How to run it, as a sweep and not as a guess:

1. **Build a SNAPSHOT function** over ~45 derived endpoints, fetched in one pass: `/library/sections`, each section's grid at each `type=`, `/library/sections/{id}/{recentlyAdded,newest,onDeck,recentlyViewed,recentlyViewedShows,unwatched,all?includeMeta=1,filters,sorts,common,firstCharacter,genre,contentRating,year,decade,resolution,userRating,label,collection,actor,duplicate}`, `/library/all`, `/library/recentlyAdded`, `/library/onDeck`, `/hubs`, `/hubs/promoted?contentDirectoryID={id}`, `/hubs/home/{recentlyAdded,onDeck,continueWatching}`, `/hubs/sections/{id}`, `/hubs/sections/{id}/continueWatching/items`, `/hubs/search?query=...`, `/library/search?query=...`, `/status/sessions/history/all?type=1`, `/playlists`, `/library/collections`, the written row's own detail, its PARENT's detail and `/children`, its GRANDPARENT's detail and `/allLeaves`, `/library/tags?type=...`, and `/library/metadata/{id}/related`. Normalise out the volatile fields (the ones `harness/scripts/replay.py`'s `VOLATILE` list names) and hash or diff the rest.
2. **For each write, snapshot -> write -> snapshot, and diff the two snapshots.** That per-write DIFF SET is the measurement. Run the identical sequence against plexsim on 32588 and **diff the DIFF SETS**, not the responses.
3. **Cover ~15 writes**, each on a freshly reset copy where it matters: a title edit on a movie; a title edit on a show; a `titleSort` edit with and without its lock; a genre add and a genre removal; a `contentRating` edit on a show; a scrobble on an episode; an unscrobble; a `/:/progress` write; a timeline beat that crosses 90%; a `userRating` write; an artwork PICK; an artwork upload; a collection create and an add; a playlist create and an add; `removeFromContinueWatching`; a library preference write; a section-path save over two rows; a delete of a collection.
4. **Report only a difference you can name.** "plexsim's Recently Added does not move after an edit and the reference's does" is a finding; "the two snapshots differ" is not.

Questions the sweep should answer, none of which anybody here has measured:

- Does an EDIT move a row in `/library/recentlyAdded`, `/hubs/home/recentlyAdded` or the section's `recentlyAdded`? Cycle 20 established an edit does not move `updatedAt` -- so does anything derived move at all?
- Does adding a genre make the new tag appear in `/library/sections/{id}/genre` immediately, with the right `count`? Does REMOVING the last row carrying a tag remove the axis entry, or leave a zero-count bucket? Does it change `/filters`? Does it reach `/library/tags`?
- Does a scrobble move `/library/onDeck`, the section's `/onDeck`, `/hubs/home/onDeck`, `/hubs/sections/{id}/continueWatching/items`, the show's `viewedLeafCount`, the season's, `/unwatched`, and `/status/sessions/history/all` -- and in what ORDER do the surviving rows come back?
- Does a title edit change what `/hubs/search` and `/library/search` find, immediately? Does it change `/library/sections/{id}/search`? Does it change `/autocomplete`? Does it change the `firstCharacter` axis and its COUNTS?
- Does an artwork write change the `composite` of a collection or a playlist that holds the row, and the parent's borrowed `parentThumb`/`grandparentThumb` versions?
- Does a collection create appear in `/library/sections/{id}/collection` as an axis entry, in `/library/tags`, in `/hubs/search`, and in the member row's `Collection` tag -- and does a DELETE take all four away?
- Does any write move `/library/sections`' own row (`updatedAt`, `scannedAt`, counts), and does it move the section's `Meta`?
- Does a write to a SEASON or an EPISODE move the SHOW's row in a listing, and its position under the default sort?
- Which of these are SYNCHRONOUS on a real PMS and which settle a second or two later? Measure the settle time and say so -- plexsim writes synchronously, so a rule that needs a delay is a recorded limit, not a defect.

Read `library.py` before you start, so you know which derived views plexsim recomputes and which it stores.

## Report format

Most important first. For each finding: the exact write (method, path, query params in order) and the exact READ that differs; **the reference's verbatim before/after for that read** (trimmed but real); plexsim's verbatim before/after; **what it costs a real client** (which screen goes stale, and for whom) -- a finding that changes nothing any client can see should say so; and which of HANDOVER.md 3.1's six failure classes your own measurement could belong to, and why it does not.

Then how many (write, endpoint) pairs you swept and how many you triaged as (a) catalog content, a version difference or the copy's own limit, (b) already recorded above, (c) a real behavioural difference -- report only (c) as findings. **If you find nothing in (c), say exactly "no findings"** and list what you compared. End with the two `refcheck7.py` comparison results.

==== Cycle 26 | Territory A | the PLAYBACK surface, read-only -- the decision, the transcode family and the Dashboard's statistics | plexsim port 32589 | READ-ONLY on kids AND zen ====

## Your territory: the PLAYBACK surface end to end -- the decision, the transcode session, the byte reads and the Dashboard rows behind them

Every dimension this programme has swept mechanically is now closed: path (cycle 14), method
and parameter (15), request and response headers (16), the row (17), the container (18), the
child element (19), the filter and sort engines against a real library (18-20), attribute order
(22), child-element order (23), **client identity (25)** and **the cross-endpoint write cascade
(25)**. Do not re-sweep any of them.

**This is the one large functional surface nobody has swept.** Cycle 6 measured the decision
container's refusals and the `/player/*` 400s; cycles 10, 14 and 15 measured the timeline's
storage grammar. Nothing has ever walked the whole playback path as a client walks it, and
nothing has swept the `/video/:/transcode/universal/*` family at all.

**Know what bounds it before you start: plexsim has no encoder.** "A valid transcode start" is
in HANDOVER.md section 8 as deliberately not implemented, and so are `transcoderVideo*` fields
and the Session block on the unclaimed copy. A finding whose only content is "the reference
transcodes and plexsim does not" is not a finding. What IS in scope is everything around that:
the shapes, the statuses, the parameter validation, the session bookkeeping, the rows the
Dashboard reads, and every path where plexsim answers something a real PMS does not answer at
all.

Read `sessions.py` and the `/video/:/transcode` and `/library/parts` handlers in `server.py`
before you start, so you know what plexsim already claims.

The territory, in the order a client actually walks it:

1. **The decision.** `GET /video/:/transcode/universal/decision` with the FULL parameter set a
   real client sends -- `path`, `mediaIndex`, `partIndex`, `protocol`, `directPlay`,
   `directStream`, `subtitles`, `audioBoost`, `location`, `mediaBufferSize`, `session`,
   `hasMDE`, `videoQuality`, `videoResolution`, `maxVideoBitrate`, `X-Plex-Session-Identifier`
   and a real `X-Plex-Platform` and `X-Plex-Client-Profile-Extra`. Sweep the PARAMETERS: which
   are required, which are parsed, which change the answer. `directPlay=0` forces a transcode
   decision on a real PMS -- what does the container carry then, and what does plexsim answer?
   What does `protocol=hls` versus `dash` versus absent change? Does the copy's answer move with
   `videoQuality` or `maxVideoBitrate`?
2. **The session.** `GET /video/:/transcode/universal/start`, `/start.mpd`, `/start.m3u8`,
   `/ping`, `/stop`, `/subtitles`, and `/video/:/transcode/universal/session/{id}/...`. Their
   statuses, their bodies, their parameter refusals, and **what each one leaves behind**: does
   a `start` create a row in `/status/sessions`, `/transcode/sessions`, `/statistics/bandwidth`?
   Does `/stop` remove it? Does a `ping` keep it alive, and what happens when one stops?
3. **The byte path.** `/library/parts/{id}/{ts}/file.ext` under Range, under no Range, with a
   bad ts, with a part id that is not there, with `download=1`, with `X-Plex-Session-Identifier`.
   plexsim's own part handler has two modes; the shapes and the headers are what matter here,
   not the bytes.
4. **What the Dashboard reads while something plays.** `/status/sessions`, `/status/sessions/
   background`, `/transcode/sessions`, `/statistics/bandwidth`, `/statistics/media`,
   `/statistics/resources`, `/activities` -- swept for shape, for what appears and disappears,
   and for the paths plexsim answers that a real PMS 404s (cycle 25 found
   `/library/sections/{id}/recentlyReleased` that way, and it had been invented).

You are READ-ONLY on kids and zen, so most of section 2 cannot be driven there -- `/start`,
`/ping` and `/stop` are all in the `WRITE_GET` list. **Measure the SHAPES you can reach
read-only** (the decision is a GET that writes nothing when it refuses; `/status/sessions` and
the statistics family are plain reads) and say clearly which parts of the territory you could
not reach and why. A bounded, honest map of this surface is worth more than a guess at the
half you cannot drive.

**Sweep it by comparing THREE servers**: fetch each path from kids, from zen and from plexsim,
and report only where the two references AGREE and plexsim differs. That filters out every
version difference automatically.

==== Cycle 26 | Territory B | the playback SESSION lifecycle as a write, and the three butler behaviours cycle 25 left unmeasured | plexsim port 32588 | WRITES to plexscratch ====

## Your territory: the playback SESSION lifecycle as a write, and the three butler behaviours cycle 25 left unmeasured

Every dimension this programme has swept mechanically is now closed: path (cycle 14), method
and parameter (15), request and response headers (16), the row (17), the container (18), the
child element (19), the filter and sort engines against a real library (18-20), attribute order
(22), child-element order (23), **client identity (25)** and **the cross-endpoint write cascade
(25)**. Do not re-sweep any of them.

You have the writable copy, so you get the half of the playback surface the read-only hunt
cannot reach — plus three butler behaviours that are measured but unconfirmed.

### 1. The session lifecycle, driven

A playback session is created by `/:/timeline`, not by a play queue or a decision (recorded).
What has never been swept is what a session LOOKS like while it lives and what ends it.

- Open one on `long` media and read `/status/sessions`, `/status/sessions/background`,
  `/transcode/sessions` and `/activities` at each step: before the first beat, on `playing`,
  on `paused`, on `buffering`, after a gap with no beat at all, and on `stopped`. What appears,
  what changes, what is removed, and **how long a session survives with no beat**.
- Drive `/video/:/transcode/universal/start`, `/ping` and `/stop` on the copy (it CAN
  transcode; plexsim cannot, and that bound is recorded — what matters is the bookkeeping, the
  statuses, the parameter refusals and what each leaves in the lists above).
- `Bandwidths` is armed by a DECISION and then present even on `stopped` (recorded). Sweep what
  else arms or disarms a block on the timeline's response.
- The copy is UNCLAIMED, so `/status/sessions/terminate` 401s and `/statistics/*` 404s and no
  `Session` block is ever carried. Those three are recorded INSTRUMENT LIMITS, not rules — do
  not report them, and do not let them stand as negatives.

### 2. The three butler behaviours cycle 25 left unmeasured

Cycle 23 recorded six; cycle 25 re-measured three of them on an uncontended socket and **two
did not reproduce** (`POST /butler/{Task}` fires its first frame at +0.0, and `/activities` is
empty the moment the run returns), while the doubled `general.db.backup` did and is now built.
These three are still only cycle 23's:

- **`CleanOldBundles` interleaves its two messages between the phases**, with independent
  counts. plexsim steps its subtitle through seven phases and reports both messages at the end.
- **`RefreshLibraries` reports BEFORE it scans, and its wrapper ends first.** plexsim scans and
  then reports.
- **`POST /butler` runs only what is DUE and steps its wrapper `done/(done+1)`**, and a second
  POST pushes one `queueRegenerated` frame and nothing else.

Measure each on a **freshly reset copy with nothing else writing to it** — that is exactly what
made two of the other three evaporate — with a control write on the same socket first, and say
how many runs agree. A refutation here is as valuable as a confirmation.

### 3. The frame sequences still held out (HANDOVER.md §3.3)

A collection's and a playlist's `/refresh` and `/unmatch` push sequences carrying `state 2` /
`metadataState: matching`. plexsim now emits that state on a season and a show unmatch, so the
state machine is no longer new — what is missing is the four sequences themselves. Re-measure
them (do not implement: report), and check whether `state 2 matching` appears anywhere else.

### 4. `/hubs/sections/{id}`'s own in-progress shelf

Cycle 25 measured that `removeFromContinueWatching` leaves
`/hubs/sections/{id}/continueWatching/items` alone on a real PMS and implemented that half. The
section's `tv.inprogress.{id}` SHELF inside `/hubs/sections/{id}` was seen to keep the row too
and was not implemented. Confirm it, and sweep what else that shelf follows.

==== Cycle 32 | Territory A | CONCURRENCY -- what a real PMS does when two things happen at once | plexsim port 32587 | WRITES to plexscratch and plexscratch42 ====

## Your territory: CONCURRENCY — what a real PMS does when two things happen at once

**You are the only agent writing this cycle**, so both writable copies are yours alone. The other
hunter is read-only.

Every measurement this project has ever made is of ONE request at a time against a quiet server.
Cycle 30 swept the same request twice; **nobody has asked what happens when two requests overlap**,
and plexsim is a `ThreadingHTTPServer` with shared mutable state, so the question is not academic.
HANDOVER §7's brief has named this dimension for two cycles and no cycle has taken it.

### The questions, in the order they are likely to pay

1. **Two writers on the same row.** Two edits of the same field, issued back to back and overlapping;
   an edit and a `/refresh`; an edit and an artwork write; a tag ADD and a tag REMOVE. Does the
   reference serialise them? Does the second answer 200 or a refusal? Which value survives, and does
   `updatedAt` move once or twice? **Read the row back after every pair** — the answer on the socket
   is not the answer in the database.
2. **A read DURING a write.** Start a slow write (a section `/refresh` over the whole library, a
   scan, an `/analyze`) and read the rows while it runs. Does a listing block, answer stale rows, or
   answer a partial set? Does `refreshing="1"` appear on the section, and does anything else change
   shape while work is in flight?
3. **Two SESSIONS on the same item.** Cycle 26 established that a decision arms a `(session,
   ratingKey)` pair. What happens when two clients play the same row at once — two `Session` rows?
   Two `Media@selected`? Does one client's `stopped` beat disarm the other? Does a terminate on one
   reach the other?
4. **The same mutation twice, concurrently.** Two identical `POST /playlists` calls; two collection
   creates with the same title; two `PUT .../items?uri=` adds of the same row. Does the reference
   make two rows, one row, or refuse the second? A duplicate-title collection is the interesting
   case: plexsim's tag table is case-insensitive.
5. **A write while a NOTIFICATION consumer is attached** (cycle 30's territory, one step on): do the
   frames of two overlapping writes interleave, or does each write's sequence stay contiguous? The
   client's reducer keys on `state 5` with no `metadataState`, so an interleaving that splits a pair
   is a real defect.
6. **Delete under read.** Delete a row while another request is listing it. Both references, both
   copies.

### How to run it

- **Drive the pairs from two threads with a barrier**, not from two sequential calls: "back to back"
  is not "at once", and the whole territory is about the overlap. Record both responses AND the row
  as it stands afterwards.
- **Repeat every interesting pair 10-20 times.** A race that resolves one way nine times out of ten
  is still a race, and the count is the finding.
- **Say which answers are the SERVER's and which are your harness's.** A timeout in your client is
  not a refusal from the server (cycle 31 lost time to exactly that: a status of 0 read as a
  behaviour when it meant "no response read").
- plexsim is the third server in every comparison. Where the references agree and plexsim differs,
  that is the finding; where the two references disagree, say so and do not pick one.

==== Cycle 32 | Territory B | the title= fold table and the decision model, from the reference files | plexsim port 32588 | READ-ONLY on kids and zen ====

## Your territory: two §3.3 entries with proven methods, and the files behind them

**You write NOTHING, anywhere.** Every request to kids and zen is a GET or a HEAD, and the other
hunter owns both writable copies this cycle. Your instruments are kids' five-clip library, zen's
3,887 real rows, and — new in cycle 31 — **the reference's own files, read over ssh**:
`ssh ben@kids docker exec plexlab-plex sh -c '…'` reads the PMS image read-only. That is not a PMS
request and it cannot alter the reference. **Read, never write**, and never touch `docker compose`.

Two HANDOVER §3.3 entries name their own method and nobody has run them. Both are yours.

### 1. The `title=` query FOLD table

Cycle 31 established that the fold applied to a `title=` query is a per-character TABLE and not NFD:
kids folds `é ï ē ī ì Ǐ ǐ` and does NOT fold `ị ĩ ı ş ę ȉ`, which no decomposition rule explains.
`scratchpad/c31/D/foldsweep.py` sweeps it by substituting each codepoint for one letter of a known
title and asking kids whether the row still matches — five rows, ~400 requests a letter. It was run
for three letters only:

* folds to `i`: `Ì Í Î Ï ì í î ï ī Ǐ ǐ` (11)
* folds to `s`: `ß Š š ſ` (4)
* folds to `l`: `Ľ ľ` (2)

**Sweep the rest of the alphabet** — every letter that appears in a kids or zen title you can use as
a probe — and produce the table. Then **hold it out**: score it against zen's own accented rows
(`Amélie`, `Bogotá`, the 21 Latin-1 rows cycle 20 counted, and the CJK/`’`/`–` rows that fold under
no model) and say how many it reproduces and how many it misses. A model fitted on one corpus must be
scored on the other (HANDOVER §10).

Two questions the sweep should answer on the way: is the table the SAME on both references (kids is
1.43.4, zen 1.42.2), and does it apply to the SORT title as well, or is `titleSort`'s eleven-character
Latin-1 table (cycle 20, re-confirmed cycle 31) genuinely a different table for a different purpose?

### 2. The decision model behind `DIRECT_PLAY_PROFILES`

Cycle 26 recorded WHICH products direct-play, get `1001` "Conversion OK", or get the `3000/2000/4005`
"neither" triple. Cycle 31 read the profile files and named the model's four inputs: resolve
`redirect=`, then `<DirectPlayProfiles>`, then `<TranscodeTargets>`, then
`<CodecProfiles><Limitations>`. Of the thirteen products cycle 26 sampled, eleven are explained
exactly; **Firefox and Microsoft Edge are not** — both declare video transcode targets that ought to
cover the item and both answer "neither".

Build the model. Read every profile's four blocks out of the image, read the ITEM's own container,
codecs, bit depth, channel count and resolution off the reference, and predict the decision for each
of the 78 profiles. Then **drive the decision endpoint** on kids and zen (a decision is a GET and is
on the guarded list — check `probes/`'s guard and use `hasMDE=1` and the plain form as cycle 26 did)
and score the prediction. Report the miss count, the misses themselves, and what separates Firefox
and Edge from the profiles the model gets right.

plexsim hard-codes two names where **45 of the 78 declare direct play**, so a model good enough to
implement is worth a great deal more than the two-name list.

### The rules of this territory

- **Score, do not spot-check.** Each deliverable is a miss count over a named population plus the
  miss rows themselves.
- **Distinguish the copy's state from the surface's rule.** kids is a five-clip library and zen is
  37% trashed rows (cycle 31's hunt B counted 2,229 of 3,887 movies carrying `deletedAt`).
- **Paging**: always send `X-Plex-Container-Start`/`-Size`, and cache what you fetch to disk so a
  re-score costs nothing.

Cycle 33's hunt reached the refresh OPENING. Cycle 34 had no hunts. Cycle 35's hunt reached the
library CREATE and the butler enabled table — and **reported the other seven items as "already
correct"**. HANDOVER §3.1 class F is "a hunter's negative taken on trust": that report is exactly
that shape, and this cycle's job is to hold it out. **Re-measure each of these against BOTH copies
and against plexsim, frame for frame**, and say for each whether it reproduces, differs, or cannot
be compared:

- **`showOrdering`'s 45-frame cascade.** `PUT /library/metadata/{show}/prefs?showOrdering=dvd` is
  recorded as pushing 45 frames (it renumbers the episodes, so the server re-reads the tree) while
  `flattenSeasons`, `episodeSort`, `useOriginalTitle` and `subtitleMode` push ZERO. Count them on
  both copies, on a show with two seasons, and compare with plexsim's. **Count the frames and name
  each one's `state`, `metadataState` and `type`** — a count alone has been wrong before.
- **The type-15/18 list PAIRS.** A playlist's frames are type 15 and a collection's 18, and a list
  edit pushes its own pair. Drive: create, rename, an item add, an item remove, a move, an artwork
  write, `/refresh`, `/unmatch`, a `prefs` write and a delete, on BOTH a playlist and a collection,
  on both copies. What does plexsim push for each?
- **A SHOW-level `/refresh`** (49 frames recorded), a SEASON's (18) and an EPISODE's (7), on both.
- **The library DELETE counts.** Cycle 30 established a library delete pushes NO per-row timeline
  frames, only eight `library.delete` activity frames. Confirm on both copies, on a library with
  ≥3 rows. (Do this LAST and reset afterwards; see SAFETY.)
- **The subtitle UPLOAD** (recorded: pushes NOTHING) and the **stream `PUT`** (recorded: accepted,
  stores nothing).
- **`/match`** and a **`?path=`-scoped section scan**.

**Design the agent confound out.** plexscratch42 is a fresh isolated install whose online agent can
never finish work, so a sequence that depends on the agent is not comparable between the copies.
Use a `tv.plex.agents.none` library on BOTH copies where the sequence touches metadata, or say
explicitly that the comparison cannot be made. Cycle 33 lost a finding to exactly this.

**How to listen.** Open ONE notification socket per copy with a real `filters=` whitelist (a socket
opened with `filters=all` receives NOTHING — `all` is not an event type), send a CONTROL write
first and confirm you see its frames, then drive. Record every frame with its full JSON. Nothing
else may be writing to the copy while you measure (cycle 23 lost the season and show cascades to a
contended socket).

## 2. A row that trips `video.separateFields` (HANDOVER §3.3, §8)

`decision.py` reads `video.separateFields` as a constant **0** where every neighbouring value comes
off the stream, and `mediainfo` never derives it. zen's only interlaced Stream sits on a stale Media
that answers `2012 File is missing` to all 78 profiles, so the refusal cannot be witnessed there
and no check can be proven fail-then-pass. **Make the row instead.**

`handover/tools/mklangless.sh` shows the shape of the answer: cycle 34 muxed two language-less audio
tracks onto plexscratch's writable media, scanned it, and drove the 78 profiles. Do the same with
an INTERLACED encode — `ffmpeg -flags +ilme+ildct -top 1` over one of the copy's clips, or
`-vf tinterlace=interleave_top -flags +ilme+ildct` — into a NEW file in the copy's writable media
directory, scan it, and check the row: does the Stream carry `scanType`/`fieldOrder`, and what does
`mediainfo` derive? Then drive all 78 profiles with `handover/tools/ddrive.py` and report:

- Which profiles refuse it, with the **verbatim refusal sentence and code**, and whether the
  refusal names `video.separateFields`.
- What `mediainfo.py` would have to derive from ffprobe to reproduce it (ffprobe names
  `field_order` on the stream: which values map to which `separateFields` value?).
- The cells in the shape `tests/fixtures/decisions/cells.jsonl` holds, so the corpus can absorb them.

Note plexscratch42's media is mounted READ-ONLY, so this belongs on **plexscratch**. If a new file
cannot be added there either, say so and say what you tried.

PMS builds a multi-part Media). Each would exercise a decision limitation the corpus does not have.

## 1. The hub matcher's fuzzy BAND — finish the measurement, then decide whether it is buildable

This is HANDOVER §3.3's oldest open search entry and cycle 35 attached the first real numbers to it.
What is established:

- A hit's SCORE has three tiers: an exact title is **0.93**, a query whose terms match the title's
  words in order starting at the title's **first** word is **0.73**, everything else is the
  **0.33** fuzzy band. (Built this cycle; do not re-report the tiers.)
- The MATCHER takes a multi-word query word by word, in order, tokenises the title on WHITESPACE,
  and matches a one-character query against a title equal to it.
- The fold is a 100-character TABLE plus a simple case fold on the query side.
- **The band itself**: a query carrying a capital macron, `İ` or `Ÿ` — characters the fold table
  deliberately does not reach — still comes back, scored by word POSITION and word LENGTH. A
  length-2 word at the query's first position drops to the 0.33 floor; the same word at the last
  position has no effect at all; words of three characters or more show none. 16 zen titles, 2 per
  length.
- The CASE passes: a capital macron fails in the FIRST word of a partial query (`Bīg Buck` → 0.33)
  and hits in a later one (`Big Būck` → 0.73), and a full-title query tolerates it anywhere except
  in a two-letter word (`Tears Ōf Steel` → 0.73).

**What is missing is the FUNCTION.** Sweep it properly and report a model with a miss count:

1. **Is the 0.33 a floor or a continuum?** Collect every distinct score a real PMS answers over a
   few hundred (query, title) pairs on zen — single-character edits at each position, transpositions,
   omitted characters, an inserted character, a wrong character, a truncated word, a query longer
   than the title — and report the SET of distinct values. If there are only three values on the
   whole surface, that is the finding and the band is buildable as a constant. If there is a
   continuum, report its shape (is it edit distance? a ratio? Jaro-Winkler? Damerau?) and fit it.
2. **What is the THRESHOLD below which no row comes back at all?** Push a query further and further
   from a title and find where the hit disappears. That boundary is what plexsim must reproduce to
   stop returning nothing where a real PMS returns a row.
3. **Does the band depend on the ROW or only on the query and title?** Try the same distortion
   against a long title and a short one, a one-word title and a five-word one.
4. **Score your model** against a HELD-OUT set: fit on kids' five titles, score on zen's, or fit on
   half of a zen sample and score on the other half. A model fitted on one corpus must be scored on
   the other (HANDOVER §10). Report the miss count and the miss rows.
5. Say plainly, at the end, **whether plexsim should carry a similarity score at all** — what it
   would cost, and what a real client gains. The client promotes a hub on the score, so a row that
   comes back at 0.33 is still a row the user sees.

Both `/hubs/search` and `/library/search` carry a score; say whether they agree on every cell.

## 2. Decision limitations no measured row trips (HANDOVER §3.3)

`tests/fixtures/decisions/cells.jsonl` now holds 1,950 measured (profile, row) cells and
`handover/tools/ddrive.py` drives them. The corpus exercises width, level, channels, bitrate,
`optimizedForStreaming` and — as of cycle 35 — a `list=` limitation and `has64bitOffsets`. **These
are still untripped**, and zen holds rows that would trip them:

- **10-bit / `high 10` beyond the one row already in the corpus**, and HDR / Dolby Vision rows
  (`<CodecProfiles>` names bit depth and colour primaries).
- **A high-channel-count audio row** (7.1, Atmos/TrueHD, DTS-HD MA) against profiles that cap
  channels.
- **A very high bitrate row**, and a row with an unusual container (`mkv` with `pgs` subtitles,
  `avi`, `wmv`, `ts`) against profiles whose `<DirectPlayProfiles>` do not list it.
- **A multi-Media row** (zen's rk=401 has fifteen) — what does a decision answer for `mediaIndex`
  1..14, and does the corpus's single-Media assumption hold?
- Anything that trips a `<Match>` on a codec `<Limitations>` block the 25 shapes in the corpus miss.

Pick rows by QUERYING zen's own columns (`videoCodec`, `audioChannels`, `bitrate`,
`videoResolution`, `hdr`, `dovi` filters, and `/library/sections/1/all?type=1&…`), read each row's
full `Media`/`Part`/`Stream` as the reference writes it, drive the 78 profiles against it with
`ddrive.py`, and **report the cells in exactly the shape `cells.jsonl` holds** so the corpus can
absorb them without a transcription step. Say, for each new row, which limitation it trips and
whether `decision.py`'s current model predicts it — a row the model already gets right is still
worth adding, and a row it gets WRONG is this cycle's most valuable finding.

**Avoid the stale-Media trap**: zen's interlaced row answers `2012 File is missing` to all 78
profiles. Check a candidate row answers a real decision before you drive all 78.

## 3. The dual-layer Dolby Vision disagreement (HANDOVER §8)

zen says a dual-layer Dolby Vision file is **ONE** video Stream (`DOVIELPresent: true`); the
recorded note in `docs/findings.md`, measured on **fun** (now out of scope), says the enhancement
layer is a **second** video stream. One witness each side. Settle it from what you can reach:

- Find every zen row carrying `DOVIELPresent` and count its video Streams. How many rows, and is
  the answer uniform?
- Does kids hold anything comparable? (Probably not — say so.)
- Is there a `profile`/`doviProfile` value that distinguishes single-layer (profile 5/8) from
  dual-layer (profile 7) rows, and does the stream count follow it? A profile-7 row with one video
  Stream would settle it against the recorded note; a profile-7 row with two would settle it for.
- Report what `mediainfo.py` currently derives and whether it would be right for zen's rows.

# Cycle 49 | Territory A | the CLAIMED-vs-UNCLAIMED census

**The axis.** Every write rule this project holds, and a large share of its read rules, was
measured on `plexscratch` or `plexscratch42` — which are **UNCLAIMED** servers, isolated from
plex.tv. `zen` and `kids` are **CLAIMED**. Nobody has ever swept the difference systematically.
Four differences are already known and recorded (`/livetv` answers 403 unclaimed and the three-row
container claimed; `/statistics/*` 404s unclaimed; `/status/sessions/terminate` 401s unclaimed; no
`Session` block is ever carried on a copy). Those four are the ones somebody happened to trip over.

**This is the project's own failure class E — an instrument limit mistaken for a server rule — and
it has never been audited.** plexsim runs `--assert-claimed`, so wherever a rule was copied off an
unclaimed server and the claimed answer differs, plexsim is wrong.

**What to do.**

1. `plexscratch42` is the controlled instrument: it is the **same build as zen (1.42.2)** and the
   same image, so a difference between them is either the claim or the library's content — and you
   can tell those apart because `plexscratch` (1.43.4) is the same image as `kids` (1.43.4) and
   gives you the second pair.
2. Enumerate a broad, finite catalog of **safe read families** — every top-level path, every section
   leaf, every item leaf, the hub paths, the `/system` subtree, `/media/providers`, `/:/prefs`,
   `/library/shared`, `/myplex/*`, `/security/*`, `/updater/*`, `/butler`, `/devices`, `/accounts`,
   `/clients*`, `/livetv/*`, `/media/*`, `/statistics/*`, `/sync*`, `/playQueues`, `/transcode/*`,
   `/photo/:/transcode`, `/:/resources/*`, `/web/*`. Derive the list from what the servers
   themselves emit (`GET /` carries 25 `Directory` rows naming the top-level endpoints) plus what
   `docs/findings.md` names, rather than from what you can think of.
3. For each family, read it on **all four servers** in both JSON and XML, with and without a token,
   and classify each difference as: (a) claim-dependent, (b) version-dependent, (c) content-
   dependent, (d) identical. Use the 2×2 (claimed/unclaimed × 1.42.2/1.43.4) to separate them —
   that separation is the whole point of the design.
4. For every claim-dependent difference, decide what **plexsim** should answer (it claims to be
   claimed) and check what it actually answers on `http://127.0.0.1:32611`.
5. Then go the other way: grep `docs/findings.md` for rules whose recorded evidence is
   "measured on the copy" / "on plexscratch" / "on plexscratch42" and check each against the
   claimed pair wherever a claimed server can answer it at all. Report every rule you find that is
   a claim artifact.

Report the family count, the four-way partition with counts, every claim-dependent difference, and
for each one whether plexsim answers the claimed form. Also report the families where the claimed
pair cannot be read at all (a write, a private surface), as explicit bounds rather than silence.


# Cycle 49 | Territory B | the CONNECTION and TRANSPORT layer

**The axis.** Cycle 37 swept the raw request *line* and the header caps; cycle 33 and cycle 50-era
work swept the response *header block*; cycle 35 swept the encoded body; cycle 32 swept two
concurrent requests released from one barrier. **Nobody has ever swept the connection itself** —
what a real PMS does with the TCP stream around and between requests.

Everything here must be driven from a **raw socket**, never `urllib`/`requests`/`curl`: every
finding in this territory is invisible to a library that owns the connection for you.

**What to measure**, on `zen` (1.42.2, the build plexsim reports) and `kids` (1.43.4), read-only, and
then against frozen plexsim at `http://127.0.0.1:32611`:

1. **Pipelining.** Two (and three) complete requests written into one socket in one `send()`, with
   no read between. Does a real PMS answer both, in order, on the one connection? What about a
   pipelined pair where the first is a 404 or a 401? What about a pipelined pair where the first
   carries `Connection: close`? plexsim advertises `X-Plex-Http-Pipeline` handling (cycle 46), so
   the answer matters.
2. **Segmentation.** One request split across two or three TCP segments with a pause between
   (header block split mid-name, mid-value, and exactly at the `\r\n\r\n`). Is there a timeout? How
   long may a client take to finish a request line / a header block / a body before the server gives
   up, and what does it answer when it does?
3. **Keep-alive semantics.** `Connection: keep-alive` and `Connection: close` on an HTTP/1.0
   request and on an HTTP/1.1 request, in both directions; an absent `Connection` on HTTP/1.0 (the
   default is close) and on HTTP/1.1 (the default is keep-alive). What does the response say and
   what does the socket actually do? The recorded rule is `Keep-Alive: timeout=20` and an idle
   socket really closed at 20 s — re-measure the boundary and find out whether the timer is idle-
   based or absolute, and whether it resets per request.
4. **Request bodies.** `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` on a request that takes a body (the subtitle
   upload on a writable copy; a query-only write). Is chunked accepted at all? A body larger than
   `Content-Length`; a body smaller than it; `Content-Length` plus `Transfer-Encoding` together; two
   `Content-Length` headers that disagree. The recorded rules are "an unparseable `Content-Length`
   is IGNORED and a negative one is a 500" and "GET and HEAD never consume a declared request body"
   — check what happens to the *next* request on the same socket in each case, which is the half
   nobody measured.
5. **Half-close and abort.** Client shuts down the write side after a complete request; client
   resets the connection mid-response (an artwork read, a container read); client disappears during
   a part read. What does the server log/do, and does anything leak (a session, a transcode, a
   counter)? Cycle 44 measured that the bandwidth statistic counts what was WRITTEN — check the
   connection-level half of that.
6. **Connection limits.** How many simultaneous connections does a real PMS accept from one peer
   before it stops accepting; what does the 101st look like? Keep this bounded and gentle on zen —
   **it is production**: a handful of sockets, held briefly, never a flood. If you cannot do it
   safely on zen, do it on `plexscratch42` and say so.
7. **`Expect: 100-continue`** on a request with a body (recorded: no `100 Continue` is sent) — what
   does the server do with the body, and with the socket, afterwards?

For each cell, record the exact bytes sent, the exact bytes received, whether the socket stayed
open, and the timing. Then check plexsim and report every difference.


# Cycle 49 | Territory C | dual-format equality, exhaustively

**The axis.** A real PMS answers the same document in XML and JSON, and this project has found four
separate bugs where plexsim's two writers disagreed (`_xml=` never reaching the JSON writer; the
`_light` marker stripped before the orderer; `json_body` stripping `null` and `[]`; and the JSON key
order being the XML order). Each was found by accident, on one path. **The census has never been
run.**

Two things are invisible to every sweep this project has already done:

* **A JSON scalar's TYPE.** XML cannot tell `"1"` from `1` from `true` from `1.0`. Known instances:
  `durationTotal`/`storageTotal` are JSON STRINGS on a section grid row; a `Common`'s `userRating`
  is `"8"` where the item row's is a number; `builtIn` is `"1"` on 1.42.2; `order` is `1000.0`; a
  `/library/search` `score` is a NUMBER in JSON and five decimals in XML; `/status/sessions` sends
  several ids as strings; `UltraBlurColors` is a LIST of one there. Those are the ones somebody
  tripped over. **Sweep it.**
* **The KEY SET and the KEY ORDER.** Which attributes each writer emits, and in what order. A real
  PMS's JSON key order IS its XML order — verify that over every family rather than over the 1,241
  pairs cycle 19 checked, and check the child-element key order too.
* **Repeated children.** `/system/agents` writes its `Language` and `MediaType` as REPEATED JSON
  KEYS rather than arrays (150 occurrences on zen, 195 on kids), so a JSON parser keeps exactly one.
  Find out whether any OTHER path does that, by parsing the raw bytes rather than with `json.loads`.

**What to do.**

1. Build the family list from the servers' own words: `GET /` names the top-level endpoints; a
   section's `Meta`/pivots name its leaves; `docs/findings.md` names the rest. Cover the item leaves,
   the section leaves, the hub paths, the axes, `/system`, `/media/providers`, `/library/shared`,
   the list surfaces, `/:/prefs`, the statistics endpoints and the write RESPONSES on a writable
   copy. Aim for the same 76-family breadth cycle 46 used and say what your denominator is.
2. For each family on `zen`, `kids` and frozen plexsim, fetch **both** formats (`Accept:
   application/json` and `Accept: application/xml`) for the *identical* request, and compare:
   attribute/key SET, key ORDER, and for each JSON scalar its TYPE. Parse JSON with a
   duplicate-key-preserving parser (`object_pairs_hook`) so a repeated key is visible.
3. Report every (family, element, attribute) where plexsim's JSON type differs from the
   reference's, every key-set difference that is not explained by content, and every ordering
   difference.
4. Distinguish content differences from shape differences: plexsim's catalog is
   `tests/catalog_ui.json`, not zen's library. Compare per (element, attribute) → set of observed
   types, never row by row.

Report the family denominator, the cell count, and the full partition. Bound explicitly anything
plexsim cannot hold (a real rating, a real tag table) rather than reporting it as a mismatch.

## What counts as a finding

A rule is a finding when a real PMS (zen for 1.42.2, backed by kids unless the difference is a
version split) answers a request one way and the frozen plexsim answers it differently, and the
difference is not catalog content. **A field only 1.43.4 sends is not a finding** (plexsim reports
1.42.2) — but a 1.42.2 behaviour plexsim lacks IS, even when kids lacks it too. A difference
explained by claim state, by catalog content (titles, counts, ids, dates, artwork URLs) or by an
entry in HANDOVER §8 is not a finding. Re-issue every candidate by hand on each server before you
report it.

# Cycle 54 | Territory A | the binary's query-parameter vocabulary | frozen plexsim 32587 | `$C/A/`

**The axis.** Cycle 46 exhausted the PREFIXED option namespace (`include*`, `exclude*`, `async*`,
`check*`, `skip*`, `only*`, `with*`: 116 names × 76 safe container families × 3 hosts). Every
other query parameter plexsim understands came from what a CLIENT was seen to send, or from the
filter grammar. **Nobody has swept the parameter names the SERVER reads.** The binary names them:
a first cut in `$C/param_like.txt` (strings followed by `=`) already shows `excludeParentID`,
`includeFields`, `includeElements`, `includeTypeFirst`, `resolveTags`, `respectTags`,
`curatedHubSeed`, `monthsAgo`, `onlyTranscodes`, `indirectMedia`, `autocorrect`, `scopeName`,
`asyncIdentifier`, `maxDistance`, `officialTitle`, `playlistType` — most of which plexsim has
never heard of.

**What to do.**

1. **Build the vocabulary mechanically, and keep its provenance.** From BOTH strings files: every
   token that appears as `name=` or `&name=` or `?name=`; plus every camelCase identifier that
   appears adjacent to (within ~40 strings of) a route template or a known parameter literal; plus
   every name the binary compares against in a param-reading context you can identify. Drop names
   cycle 46 already swept (the seven prefixes above) and names that are clearly SQL columns,
   ffmpeg options, environment variables or C++ symbols. Record for each name: kids-only, zen-only
   or both (a 1.43.4-only name is a version split, not a finding). Aim for a finite list of a few
   hundred and say exactly how you cut it.
2. **Build the family catalog.** Every distinct safe GET container family plexsim and the
   references answer: section grid, each subset leaf, every axis, `/library/all`, the item detail
   read and its leaves (`children`, `allLeaves`, `grandchildren`, `related`, `similar`, `extras`,
   `tree`, `posters`, …), every `/hubs/*` path, search endpoints, playlists and their items,
   collections, `/status/sessions/history/all`, `/statistics/*`, `/:/prefs`, `/media/providers`,
   `/system/agents`, `/library/tags`, `/playQueues/{id}` (READ of an existing queue on plexsim and
   the scratch copies only — creating a queue on kids/zen is forbidden), `/butler` (GET), etc. Use
   the same ids on each server that denote the same row TYPE. ~70–90 families.
3. **Sweep.** For each (name × family) cell, on zen (1.42.2) and on the frozen plexsim at
   `http://127.0.0.1:32587`, compare the response to that family's baseline with the name absent,
   using a few source-shaped values (`1`, `0`, a plausible id, a plausible word). Duplicate each
   baseline so a moving path (a random shelf, a timestamp) is identified before it is blamed on a
   name. A name "moves" a family when status, root, container attributes, row set, row order, or
   row projection changes. Use kids as the second witness for every mover, and plexscratch42 for
   any mover whose grammar you need to probe with a write-free but state-dependent request.
   **Page every zen request.** Budget: this is a large sweep — batch it, rate-limit it (≤ 5 req/s to
   zen) and checkpoint it to disk so a crash does not restart it.
4. **Triage every mover.** For each name that moves a reference family: does plexsim move the same
   way? If not, characterise the rule by hand (value grammar: what parses, what is refused and with
   which status; what it does to rows) on both references, and write the regression check.
   Also report the inverse — any name plexsim reacts to that no reference does.

Report the vocabulary size and how it was derived, the family count, the cell count, the mover
set with its partition (both references / 1.42.2-only / 1.43.4-only / claim-dependent), and every
finding as specified in the preamble.
Read `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-bensarmiento-debridmediamanager-plexsim/8c029687-4cba-4d75-bc2d-78af7d5bc4db/scratchpad/c54/preamble.md` first and follow it. Your territory, port and directory:

# Cycle 54 | Territory B | the binary's own SQL as the oracle for what a listing SELECTS | frozen plexsim 32588 | `$C/B/`

**The axis.** Fifty cycles fitted plexsim's listing, hub, subset, On Deck, statistics, history and
search behaviour to what the references ANSWERED, one request at a time. The server binary carries
the SQL it actually RUNS — the strings files are full of it (`select … from metadata_items …`,
`order by …`, `where … and …`, `metadata_items.title like '%`, `(metadata_items.rating_count= or …`,
`&beginsAt>=now&sort=`, hub definitions, the play-queue generator, view-history rollups,
`metadata_item_views`, `metadata_item_settings`, `statistics_*`). Cycle 53 used the database
TEMPLATE's schema to name columns; **nobody has read the statements.** A statement is a rule
written down by the server itself: which rows a hub selects, how NULLs sort, which column a sort
key reads, what joins to what, what a subset excludes, how a count is computed, what the
tiebreaker is.

**What to do.**

1. **Extract every SQL statement and SQL fragment** from both strings files (select/insert/update/
   delete/with, and clause fragments: `order by`, `where`, `group by`, `having`, `join`, `case
   when`, `coalesce`, `ifnull`, `collate`, `limit`). Keep the kids/zen split. Classify each by the
   API surface it feeds — read fragments are what matter; write statements are useful only to
   explain what a write changes. Say how many you extracted and how many you classified as
   API-reachable reads.
2. **For each API-reachable read, derive the rule it states and a request that discriminates it**
   — especially edges nobody measured: NULL ordering and NULL membership, secondary/tertiary sort
   keys, exclusions (`metadata_type != …`, `deleted_at is null`, `extra_type`, `index>0`,
   `viewed_at > …`), time windows (`monthsAgo`, `beginsAt>=now`, `at > ?`), caps (`limit ?`),
   aggregates (`count(distinct …)`, `sum`, `max(viewed_at)`), and the joins that decide which
   parent a leaf rolls up to. Hubs and subsets are the richest target: every `hubIdentifier`
   whose rows are SELECTed by a statement you can read.
3. **Measure the discriminating request on zen (1.42.2, real library, the primary witness) and kids
   (1.43.4)**, then on the frozen plexsim at `http://127.0.0.1:32588`. Where the edge needs STATE
   the references do not have (a watched episode, a rating, an in-progress offset), make that state
   on plexscratch42 (1.42.2) or plexscratch with a written undo, never on kids or zen. A rule the
   SQL states but no reference can be made to show is an explicit bound, not a finding.
4. **Hold plexsim's own implementation against each statement** — `filters.py`, `library.py`,
   `hierarchy.py`, the hub and subset builders in `server.py`. Where plexsim computes a thing the
   SQL computes differently (a different tiebreak, NULLs on the other side, a missing exclusion,
   a different window), that is a candidate; confirm it with a request before reporting it.

Report the statement count and classification, the rule set you derived, the discriminating
requests you ran (with their results on each server), and every finding as specified in the
preamble. Where the SQL overturns something recorded in `docs/findings.md` or `CLAUDE.md`, say
which bullet and why — that is a class-A/B/D overturn and the most valuable thing you can report.

## What counts as a finding

A rule is a finding when a real PMS (zen for 1.42.2, backed by kids unless the difference is a
version split) answers a request one way and the frozen plexsim answers it differently, and the
difference is not catalog content. **A field only 1.43.4 sends is not a finding** (plexsim reports
1.42.2) — but a 1.42.2 behaviour plexsim lacks IS, even when kids lacks it too. A difference
explained by claim state, by catalog content (titles, counts, ids, dates, artwork URLs) or by an
entry in HANDOVER §8 is not a finding. Re-issue every candidate by hand on each server before you
report it.

## What counts as a finding

A rule is a finding when a real PMS (zen for 1.42.2, backed by kids unless the difference is a
version split) answers a request one way and the frozen plexsim answers it differently, and the
difference is not catalog content. **A field only 1.43.4 sends is not a finding** (plexsim reports
1.42.2) — but a 1.42.2 behaviour plexsim lacks IS, even when kids lacks it too. A difference
explained by claim state, by catalog content (titles, counts, ids, dates, artwork URLs) or by an
entry in HANDOVER §8 is not a finding. Re-issue every candidate by hand on each server before you
report it.

file's own voice, citing "Cycle 57" and the references.
